Green’s function estimates for long-range quasi-periodic operators on ℤ d \mathbb{Z}^{d} and applications
Li Wen, Yuan Wu

Abstract
. We establish quantitative Green’s function estimates for a class of quasi-periodic (QP) operators on ℤ d \mathbb{Z}^{d} with certain slowly decaying long-range hopping and analytic cosine type potentials. As applications, we prove the arithmetic spectral localization, and obtain upper bounds on quantum dynamics for all phase parameters. To deal with quantum dynamics estimates, we develop an approach employing separation property (rather than the sublinear bound) of resonant blocks in the regime of Green’s function estimates.



1.
Introduction

The study of long-range hopping QP operators has attracted great attention over the years, cf. e.g.,
[
BJ02
,
JK16
,
GY20
,
JLS20
,
JL21
,
Shi22
,
Liu22
,
SW22
,
GYZ23
,
Liu23
,
Shi23
,
SS23
,
SW23
,
SW24
,
WXZ25
]
.
In this paper, we are concerned with long-range QP operators

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}(\theta)=\varepsilon\mathcal{W}_{\phi}+v(\theta+\bm{n}\cdot\bm{\omega})\delta_{\bm{n},\bm{n}^{\prime}},\ \bm{n},\bm{n}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d},

(1.1)

where the off-diagonal part (i.e., the hopping term)
\mathcal{W}_{\phi}
is a Toeplitz operator satisfying

\displaystyle(\mathcal{W}_{\phi}\psi)(\bm{n})=\sum_{\bm{l}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\phi(\bm{n}-\bm{l})\psi(\bm{l}),\ \phi(\bm{0})=0,\ |\phi(\bm{n})|\leq e^{-\alpha\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{n}\|)}

(1.2)

with some
\alpha>0
,
\rho>1
and
\|\bm{n}\|=\sup\limits_{1\leq i\leq d}|n_{i}|
. The potential
v
is an analytic cosine type function (cf. (
1.3
) for details). Note that the hopping term
\mathcal{W}_{\phi}
had previously been used in the construction of almost-periodic solutions for some nonlinear Hamiltonian equations
[
Pös90
]
, and was recently introduced by Shi-Wen
[
SW22
]
and Shi-Wen-Yan
[
SWY25
]
in the study of the localization problems for operators on
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
with monotone quasi-periodic potentials and random potentials, respectively. We also mention that the existence of localized eigenfunctions whose decay rate is the same as (
1.2
) has been established in physics
[
CRBLD17
]
. The present work aims to prove establish quantitative Green’s function estimates for (
1.1
) via multi-scale analysis (MSA) scheme in the spirit of Fröhlich-Spencer-Wittwer
[
FSW90
]
, Bourgain
[
Bou00
]
and Cao-Shi-Zhang
[
CSZ24b
]
. As applications, we prove the arithmetic localization and obtain upper bounds on quantum dynamics for all phase parameters.

The groundbreaking work of Fröhlich-Spencer-Wittwer
[
FSW90
]
was the first to extend the Green’s function estimation method based on multi-scale analysis (MSA) from random potentials
[
FS83
]
to QP potentials. This method is primarily based on eigenvalue variational techniques, thus requiring the operators to be self-adjoint and the potentials to be single-variable functions. In 2000, Bourgain
[
Bou00
]
made a breakthrough by proposing an MSA-type Green’s function estimation method that does not rely on eigenvalue variation techniques: the resonances are entirely determined by the zeros of the Dirichlet determinant based on the preparation theorem. Subsequently, Bourgain and collaborators
[
BGS02
,
Bou07
]
incorporated matrix-valued Cartan’s estimates and semi-algebraic set theory into the MSA framework, enabling them to derive large deviation theorems (LDT) for the finite volume Green’s functions of multi-dimensional QP operators with multivariate analytic potentials. The result of
[
Bou07
]
was later extended by Jitormirskaya-Liu-Shi
[
JLS20
]
to operators with arbitrary multi-frequency and exponentially long-range hopping. In 2024, Cao-Shi-Zhang
[
CSZ24b
]
successfully generalized Bourgain’s method
[
Bou00
]
to arbitrary-dimensional lattice
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
case. Meanwhile, the eigenvalue-variation-based Green’s function estimation method
[
FSW90
]
was also extended by Cao-Shi-Zhang
[
CSZ23
,
CSZ24a
]
to the
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
case: they overcame the challenges posed by level crossing phenomena.

In fact, the Green’s function estimates actually allow us to study Anderson localization (i.e., the pure point spectrum with exponentially decaying eigenfunctions). To derive localization, it is necessary to remove certain parameters (
\omega
or
\theta
) to overcome the double resonance phenomenon. The proof of localization via LDT-type Green’s functions estimate methods typically requires removing a positive-measure set (depending on
\theta
) of
\omega
, resulting in a non-arithmetic result. To achieve arithmetic localization (the removed sets for both
\omega
and
\theta
in the localization proof admit an explicit arithmetic description), precise characterization of resonances is required. In higher dimensions,
[
JK16
,
GY20
]
first achieved arithmetic localization for QP operators with the cosine potential via reducibility arguments based on the Aubry duality. Recently, Cao-Shi-Zhang
[
CSZ23
,
CSZ24a
,
CSZ24b
]
proposed an innovative approach to proving arithmetic localization based on Green’s function estimates, which enabled the demonstration of arithmetic localization for multi-dimensional QP operators with
C^{2}
-cosine like potentials. All the aforementioned arithmetic localization results require operators with exponentially long-range hopping. The primary objective of this work is to verify arithmetic localization for multi-dimensional QP operators featuring slower-decaying (cf. (
1.2
)) long-range hopping.

Cao-Shi-Zhang
[
CSZ23
,
CSZ24a
,
CSZ24b
]
even established arithmetic dynamical localization for these operators. In particular, for fixed Diophantine frequency
\omega
and a.e.
\theta\in\mathbb{T}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left(\left(|\mathscr{X}_{\mathcal{H}(\theta)}|_{\delta_{\bm{0}}}^{p}\right)(t)\right)<+\infty\text{ (cf. \eqref{pthm} for details)}.

A natural question arises: can one demonstrate upper bounds of
\left(|\mathscr{X}_{\mathcal{H}(\theta)}|_{\delta_{\bm{0}}}^{p}\right)(t)
in
t
(quantum dynamics estimates) for all
\theta\in\mathbb{T}
? This problem has been resolved by
[
JL21
,
SS23
,
Liu23
]
in the case of exponentially long-range QP operators. The methodologies in these works are fundamentally built upon large deviation estimates of Green’s functions, thus inherently requiring the long-range hopping to exhibit exponential decay. Another motivation of the present work is to develop a novel strategy based on the separation property of resonant blocks (rather than LDT) for investigating quantum dynamical estimates, which enables the treatment of long-range hopping of type (
1.2
). In fact, building upon Green’s function estimates of
[
SW24
]
, our method can handle quantum dynamical estimates for operators with power-law long-range hopping.

1.1.
Main results

Recalling (
1.1
),
the potential
v
is an analytic function defined on

\displaystyle\mathbb{D}_{R}=\left\{z\in\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{Z}:\ |\Im z|\leq R\right\},\ R>0,

satisfying the following Morse condition: there exist
\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}>0
such that

\displaystyle\kappa_{1}\|z_{1}-z_{2}\|_{\mathbb{T}}\|z_{1}+z_{2}\|_{\mathbb{T}}\leq|v(z_{1})-v(z_{2})|\leq\kappa_{2}\|z_{1}-z_{2}\|_{\mathbb{T}}\|z_{1}+z_{2}\|_{\mathbb{T}},

(1.3)

for all
z_{1},z_{2}\in\mathbb{D}_{R}
, where the torus norm is defined by

\displaystyle\|z\|_{\mathbb{T}}=\sqrt{\|\Re z\|_{\mathbb{T}}^{2}+|\Im z|^{2}},\ \|x\|_{\mathbb{T}}=\inf_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}|l-x|\ \text{for}\ x\in\mathbb{R}.

We let
\theta\in\mathbb{T}=\mathbb{R}/\mathbb{Z}
,
\bm{\omega}\in[0,1]^{d}
, and
\bm{n}\cdot\bm{\omega}=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{d}n_{i}\omega_{i}.

In the following, we assume that
\bm{\omega}\in{DC}_{\tau,\gamma}
for some
\tau>d
,
\gamma>0
with

\displaystyle{DC}_{\tau,\gamma}=\left\{\bm{\omega}\in[0,1]^{d}:\ \|\bm{n}\cdot\bm{\omega}\|_{\mathbb{T}}\geq\frac{\gamma}{\|\bm{n}\|^{\tau}}\ {\rm for}\ \forall\bm{n}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\setminus\{\bm{0}\}\right\}.

(1.4)

1.1.1.
Quantitative Green’s function estimates

The main result of this paper is a quantitative version of Green’s function estimates, which will
imply both arithmetic spectral localization and sub-polynomial bounds of moments.

We first introduce the function class of potentials. For fixed
R>0
, let
\mathscr{V}_{R}
be the class of analytic functions
v:\mathbb{D}_{R}\to\mathbb{C}
satisfying the following uniform condition: there exist positive constants
\kappa_{1}=\kappa_{1}(v,R)
and
\kappa_{2}=\kappa_{2}(v,R)
such that inequality (
1.3
) holds for all
z_{1},z_{2}\in\mathbb{D}_{R}
. Let
|v|_{R}=\sup\limits_{z\in\mathbb{D}_{R}}|v(z)|
for
v\in\mathscr{V}_{R}
and
\mathscr{V}=\bigcup_{R>0}\mathscr{V}_{R}
. We remark that more details about
\mathscr{V}
can be found in
[
SW24
]
.

Given
E\in\mathbb{C}
and
\Lambda\subset\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, the Green’s function (if exists) is defined by

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(E;\theta)=(\mathcal{H}_{\Lambda}(\theta)-E)^{-1},\ \mathcal{H}_{\Lambda}(\theta)=\mathcal{R}_{\Lambda}\mathcal{H}(\theta)\mathcal{R}_{\Lambda},

where
\mathcal{H}(\theta)
is given by (
1.1
) and
\mathcal{R}_{\Lambda}
denotes the restriction operator.

Recall that
\bm{\omega}\in DC_{\tau,\gamma}
and
\rho>1
. We fix a constant
\rho^{\prime}
so that

\displaystyle 1<\rho^{\prime}<\rho<\rho^{\prime}+1.

At the s-th iteration step, let
\delta_{s}^{-1}
(resp.
N_{s}
) describe the resonance strength (resp. the size of resonant blocks) defined by

\displaystyle N_{s+1}=\left[e^{\left|\log\delta_{s}\right|^{\frac{1}{\rho^{\prime}}}}\right],\ \delta_{s+1}=\delta_{s}^{10^{5\rho^{\prime}}},\ \delta_{0}=\varepsilon_{0}^{\frac{1}{10}},

where
[x]
denotes the interger part of
x\in\mathbb{R}
.

Then we have

Theorem 1.1
.

Let
1<\rho^{\prime}<\rho<1+\rho^{\prime}
,
\bm{\omega}\in DC_{\tau,\gamma}
and
v\in\mathscr{V}_{R}
. Then there is some
\varepsilon_{0}=\varepsilon_{0}(d,\tau,\gamma,v,R,\alpha,\rho,\rho^{\prime})>0
so that for
0<|\varepsilon|\leq\varepsilon_{0}
and
E\in v(\mathbb{D}_{R/2})
, there exists a sequence

\displaystyle\{\theta_{s}=\theta_{s}(E)\}_{s=0}^{s^{\prime}}\subset\mathbb{C}\ (s^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{+\infty\})

with the following properties. Fix any
\theta\in\mathbb{T}
, if a subset
\Lambda\subset\mathbb{Z}^{d}
is
s
-
{\rm good}
(cf.
(\bm{e})_{s}
of Statement
4.1
for the definition of
s
-
{\rm good}
set, and §
4
for the definitions of
\{\theta_{s}\}_{s=0}^{s^{\prime}}
, sets
P_{s}
,
\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s}
and
\tilde{\zeta}_{s}>0
), then

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(E;\theta)\|<2\delta_{s-1}^{-3}\cdot\sup\limits_{\{\bm{k}\in P_{s}:\ \tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s}\subset\Lambda\}}(\|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}-\theta_{s}\|_{\mathbb{T}}^{-1}\cdot\|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}+\theta_{s}\|_{\mathbb{T}}^{-1})<\delta_{s}^{-3},

\displaystyle|\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(E;\theta)(\bm{x},\bm{y})|<e^{-\frac{1}{2}\alpha\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|)}\ \text{for $\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|>10\tilde{\zeta}_{s}$}.

Let us refer to §
4
for a complete description of Green’s function estimates.

1.1.2.
Arithmetic spectral localization

In this part, we state our arithmetic spectral localization result. We assume
\mathcal{H}(\theta)
is self-adjoint for
\theta\in\mathbb{T}
.

Theorem 1.2
.

Define

\displaystyle\Theta=\left\{\theta\in\mathbb{T}:\ \|2\theta+\bm{n}\cdot\bm{\omega}\|_{\mathbb{T}}\leq\frac{1}{\|\bm{n}\|^{\tau}}\ {\rm holds\ for\ finitely\ many\ }\bm{n}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\right\}.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
1.1
, for
\theta\in\mathbb{T}\setminus\Theta
,
\mathcal{H}(\theta)
has pure point spectrum and there exists a complete system of eigenfunctions
\psi=\{\psi(\bm{n})\}_{\bm{n}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}
satisfying

\displaystyle|\psi(\bm{n})|<e^{-\frac{\alpha}{4\cdot 10^{6\rho}}\log^{\rho}\left(1+\|\bm{n}\|\right)}\text{ for $\|\bm{n}\|\gg 1$}.

1.1.3.
Sub-polymonial bounds of moments

In this part, we state our quantum dynamics results. We also assume
\mathcal{H}(\theta)
is self-adjoint for
\theta\in\mathbb{T}
.

For
\psi\in\ell^{2}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
and
p>0
, let
(|\mathscr{X}_{\mathcal{H}(\theta)}|_{\psi}^{p})(t)
be the
p
-th moment of
\mathcal{H}(\theta)
,

\displaystyle\left(|\mathscr{X}_{\mathcal{H}(\theta)}|_{\psi}^{p}\right)(t)=\sum_{\bm{n}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}(1+\|\bm{n}\|)^{p}\left|\langle e^{-\sqrt{-1}t\mathcal{H}(\theta)}\psi,\delta_{\bm{n}}\rangle\right|^{2},

(1.5)

and
(|\bar{\mathscr{X}}_{\mathcal{H}(\theta)}|_{\psi}^{p})(T)
be the time-average
p
-th moment of
\mathcal{H}(\theta)
,

\displaystyle\left(|\bar{\mathscr{X}}_{\mathcal{H}(\theta)}|_{\psi}^{p}\right)(T)=\frac{2}{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{2t}{T}}\sum_{\bm{n}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}(1+\|\bm{n}\|)^{p}\left|\langle e^{-\sqrt{-1}t\mathcal{H}(\theta)}\psi,\delta_{\bm{n}}\rangle\right|^{2}dt.

(1.6)

Then we have

Theorem 1.3
.

Let
p>0
. Under the assumptions of Theorem
1.1
, there exists
T_{0}=T_{0}(d,\tau,\gamma,v,R,\alpha,\rho,\rho^{\prime},p)>0
such that for any
\theta\in\mathbb{T}
and
t,T\geq T_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle\left(|\mathscr{X}_{\mathcal{H}(\theta)}|_{\delta_{\bm{0}}}^{p}\right)(t)\leq 2^{p}e^{p(\log t)^{\frac{2}{1+\rho^{\prime}}}}

and

\displaystyle\left(|\bar{\mathscr{X}}_{\mathcal{H}(\theta)}|_{\delta_{\bm{0}}}^{p}\right)(T)\leq 2^{p}e^{p(\log T)^{\frac{2}{1+\rho^{\prime}}}}.

1.2.
Ideas of the proof and new ingredients

Our proof of Theorem
1.1
is based on a MSA type induction and combines ideas from
[
Bou00
,
CSZ24b
,
SW24
,
SWY25
]
. Once Theorem
1.1
was established, the proof of arithmetic localization just follow from standard argument. Building upon Theorem
1.1
, we also develop a new scheme to prove sub-polynomial bounds of moments by using separation property of resonant blocks.

The
\ell^{2}
-norm estimates for the Green’s function based on the similar induction hypothesis of Theorem
1.1
have already established in
[
Bou00
,
CSZ24b
,
SW24
]
. In this work, we primarily employ the methods developed in
[
SWY25
]
to handle the off-diagonal decay. While our method here is motivated by
[
SWY25
]
, there are some major differences: (1). In
[
SWY25
]
, by adopting the induction hypothesis from
[
DK89
]
, the authors only need to handle ensembles of two adjacent scales. Since we are dealing with QP case, one needs to handle the ensemble of all scales at each step. The iterative framework in
[
SWY25
]
, when applied directly, proves insufficient to achieve our target decay rate; (2). Although the quasi-metric property (cf. (
3.1
) for details) is utilized in both this work and
[
SWY25
]
, we emphasize that
n\approx\frac{\log^{\rho}N_{k+1}}{\log^{\rho}N_{k}}
in
[
SWY25
]
which can be controlled by scale-independent constant but
n\approx\frac{log^{\rho}N_{k+1}}{\log^{\rho}N_{1}}
maybe present in this work. In contrast to previous studies, we demonstrate the intrinsic necessity of term
C(\rho)\log^{\rho}n
.

In previous works, the proof of Combes-Thomas estimates invariably required the long-range hopping term to take the form
e^{f(\bm{x},\bm{y})}
, where
f(\bm{x},\bm{y})
is a metric. However, the function
f(\bm{x},\bm{y})=\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|)

(\rho>1)
is not a metric. Fortunately, it was proved in
[
SWY25
]
that the function
f(\bm{x},\bm{y})
is a quasi-metric. By exploiting quasi-metric property, we construct customized Combes-Thomas type estimates for the present framework.

Finally, we conclude by noting a crucial distinction: while
[
Liu23
]
and this work share analogous approaches to bound of moments, our construction of good sets follows a divergent path. In
[
Liu23
]
, for any
\|\bm{n}\|\gg 1
, the author relies on the large deviation theorems, postulating the existence of a neighborhood of
\bm{n}
satisfying the sublinear bound property. However, in this paper, we can obtain a good set containing
\bm{n}
by extending a origin neighborhood of
\bm{n}
directly (cf. Lemma
A.4
for details).

1.3.
Structure of the paper

The paper is organized as follows. In §
2
, we introduce some useful notations. The §
3
contains some important properties. In §
4
, we complete the proof of Theorem
1.1
. In §
5
-§
6
, we will apply quantitative Green’s function estimates to prove spectral locaolization and sub-polynomial bounds of moments. Some useful estimates are given in Appendix
A
.

2.
The notation

•

The determinant of a matrix
M
is denoted by
\det M
.

•

If
a\in\mathbb{R}
, let
\|a\|_{\mathbb{T}}={\rm dist}(a,\mathbb{Z})=\inf\limits_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}|l-a|
. For
z=a+\sqrt{-1}b\in\mathbb{C}
with
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
, define
\|z\|_{\mathbb{T}}=\sqrt{\|a\|_{\mathbb{T}}^{2}+|b|^{2}}
.

•

For
\bm{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, let

\displaystyle\|\bm{n}\|=\sup_{1\leq i\leq d}|n_{i}|.

Denote by
{\rm dist}(\cdot,\cdot)
the distance induced by
\|\cdot\|
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and define

\displaystyle{\rm diam}(\Lambda)=\sup_{\bm{k},\bm{k}^{\prime}\in\Lambda}\|\bm{k}-\bm{k}^{\prime}\|.

Given
\bm{n}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
,
\Lambda^{\prime}\subset\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}^{d}
and
L>0
, define

\displaystyle\Lambda_{L}(\bm{n})=\left\{\bm{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}:\ \|\bm{k}-\bm{n}\|\leq L\right\}

and

\displaystyle\Lambda_{L}(\Lambda^{\prime})=\left\{\bm{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}:\ {\rm dist}(\bm{k},\Lambda^{\prime})\leq L\right\}.

In particular, write
\Lambda_{L}=\Lambda_{L}(\bm{0})
.

•

For
\eta>0
, we define

\displaystyle D(\eta)=\sum_{\bm{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}e^{-\eta\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{k}\|)}<+\infty.

(2.1)

•

\{\delta_{\bm{x}}\}_{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}
is the standard basis of
\ell^{2}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
.

•

\mathcal{I}
typically denotes the identity operator.

•

\mathcal{R}_{\Lambda}
is the restriction operator with
\Lambda\subset\mathbb{Z}^{d}
.

•

Let
\mathcal{T}:\ \ell^{2}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})\to\ell^{2}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
be a linear operator. Denote by
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
the standard inner product on
\ell^{2}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
. Set
\mathcal{T}(\bm{x},\bm{y})=\langle\delta_{\bm{x}},\mathcal{T}\delta_{\bm{y}}\rangle
. The spectrum of operator
\mathcal{T}
is denoted by
\sigma(\mathcal{T})
. Finally, we define
\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}=\mathcal{R}_{\Lambda}\mathcal{T}\mathcal{R}_{\Lambda}
and
\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{*}
the adjugate operator of
\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}
, where
\Lambda\subset\mathbb{Z}^{d}.

3.
Preliminaries

3.1.
Quasi-metric property

Lemma 3.1
.

For
x_{i}\geq 0
,
1\leq i\leq n
, we have

\displaystyle\log^{\rho}\left(1+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}x_{i}\right)\leq\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\log^{\rho}(1+x_{i})+C(\rho)\log^{\rho}n,

where
C(\rho)>0
is some constant depending only on
\rho>1
.

Proof.

For a detailed proof, we refer to
[
SWY25
]
.
∎

Remark 3.1
.

We have the
quasi-metric
property: for any
\bm{x}_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
(
1\leq i\leq n
),

\displaystyle\log^{\rho}\left(1+\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{x}_{i}\right\|\right)\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}_{i}\|)+C(\rho)\log^{\rho}n.

(3.1)

3.2.
Extract lemma

Given the frequent need to extract
\log^{\rho}(1+x)
from
\log^{\rho}(1+x-y)
in the regime
x\gg y\gg 1
, we establish the following lemma:

Lemma 3.2
.

Let
x>y>0
and
1+x>2y
. Then

\displaystyle\log^{\rho}(1+x-y)\geq\left(1-2\rho\frac{y}{(1+x)\log(1+x)}\right)\log^{\rho}(1+x).

(3.2)

Proof.

We refer to the Appendix
A
for a detailed proof.
∎

3.3.
Hadamard type estimate

We need to estimate
\ell^{2}
-norm of the inverse of some operator
\mathcal{S}_{\Lambda}
. By the Cramer’s rule,
\mathcal{S}_{\Lambda}^{-1}=(\det\mathcal{S}_{\Lambda})^{-1}S_{\Lambda}^{*}
, where
\mathcal{S}_{\Lambda}^{*}(\bm{i},\bm{j})
is a determinant for
\bm{i},\bm{j}\in\Lambda
. Therefore, we can apply Hadamard’s inequality to estimate
\ell^{2}
-norm of
\mathcal{S}_{\Lambda}^{*}
and thus that of
\mathcal{S}_{\Lambda}^{-1}
.

Lemma 3.3

(Hadamard’s estimate)
.

Let
\mathcal{S}:\ \ell^{2}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})\to\ell^{2}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
be a linear operator, and
\Lambda\subset\mathbb{Z}^{d}
a finite subset. Then for any
\bm{i},\bm{j}\in\Lambda
, we have

\displaystyle|\langle\delta_{\bm{i}},\mathcal{S}_{\Lambda}^{*}\delta_{\bm{j}}\rangle|\leq\left(\sup_{\bm{x}\in\Lambda}\sum_{\bm{y}\in\Lambda}|\langle\delta_{\bm{x}},\mathcal{S}_{\Lambda}\delta_{\bm{y}}\rangle|\right)^{\#\Lambda-1}.

Moreover,

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{S}_{\Lambda}^{*}\|\leq(\#\Lambda)\cdot\left(\sup_{\bm{x}\in\Lambda}\sum_{\bm{y}\in\Lambda}|\langle\delta_{\bm{x}},\mathcal{S}_{\Lambda}\delta_{\bm{y}}\rangle|\right)^{\#\Lambda-1}.

Proof.

We refer to the Appendix
A
for a detailed proof.
∎

3.4.
The Combes-Thomas type estimate

Aiming to deal with energies outside the spectrum, we need the Combes-Thomas type estimate.

Lemma 3.4
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a self-adjoint operator in
\ell^{2}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
and
\mathcal{G}(z):=(\mathcal{A}-z)^{-1}
be the Green’s function of
\mathcal{A}
if it exists. If for some
\lambda>0
,

\displaystyle S_{\lambda}:=\sup_{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\sum_{\bm{y}\neq\bm{x}}|\mathcal{A}(\bm{x},\bm{y})|e^{\lambda\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|)}<\infty,

(3.3)

then for energies
z
not in the spectrum,
\mathscr{D}:={\rm dist}(z,\sigma(\mathcal{A}))>0
, we have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{G}(z)(\bm{x},\bm{y})|\leq\frac{e^{-\lambda^{\prime}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|)}}{\mathscr{D}-2e^{\lambda^{\prime}C(\rho)\log^{\rho}2}\cdot S_{\lambda^{\prime}}}

(3.4)

for any
\lambda^{\prime}\leq\lambda
with
2e^{\lambda^{\prime}C(\rho)\log^{\rho}2}\cdot S_{\lambda^{\prime}}<\mathscr{D}
and all
\bm{x},\bm{y}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
.

Proof.

We refer to the Appendix
A
for a detailed proof.
∎

4.
Quantitative Green’s function estimates

In this section, we fix

\displaystyle\theta\in\mathbb{D}_{R/2},\ E\in v(\mathbb{D}_{R/2}).

Write

\displaystyle E=v(\theta_{0})

(4.1)

for some
\theta_{0}\in\mathbb{D}_{R/2}
. Consider

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}(E;\theta)=\mathcal{H}(\theta)-E=\mathcal{D}+\varepsilon\mathcal{W}_{\phi},

(4.2)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{D}_{\bm{n}}\delta_{\bm{n},\bm{n}^{\prime}},\ \mathcal{D}_{\bm{n}}=v(\theta+\bm{n}\cdot\bm{\omega})-E.

For simplicity, we may omit the dependence of
\mathcal{T}(E;\theta)
on
E,\theta
and that of
\mathcal{W}_{\phi}
on
\phi
, respectively.

Now we introduce the statement of our main result on the MSA type Green’s function estimates. Define the induction parameters

\displaystyle N_{s+1}=\left[e^{\left|\log\delta_{s}\right|^{\frac{1}{\rho^{\prime}}}}\right],\ \delta_{s+1}=\delta_{s}^{10^{5\rho^{\prime}}},\ \delta_{0}=\varepsilon_{0}^{\frac{1}{10}}.

(4.3)

Thus

\displaystyle N_{s}^{10^{5}}-1\leq N_{s+1}\leq(N_{s}+1)^{10^{5}}

and

\displaystyle e^{-\log^{\rho^{\prime}}(N_{s+1}+1)}\leq\delta_{s}\leq e^{-\log^{\rho^{\prime}}N_{s+1}}.

We first introduce the following induction statement.

Statement 4.1

(called
\mathscr{P}_{s}\ (s\geq 1)
)
.

Let

\displaystyle Q_{s-1}^{\pm}

\displaystyle=\left\{\bm{k}\in P_{s-1}:\ \|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}\pm\theta_{s-1}\|_{\mathbb{T}}<\delta_{s-1}\right\},\ Q_{s-1}=Q_{s-1}^{+}\cup Q_{s-1}^{-},

\displaystyle\tilde{Q}_{s-1}^{\pm}

\displaystyle=\left\{\bm{k}\in P_{s-1}:\ \|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}\pm\theta_{s-1}\|_{\mathbb{T}}<\delta_{s-1}^{\frac{1}{100}}\right\},\ \tilde{Q}_{s-1}=\tilde{Q}_{s-1}^{+}\cup\tilde{Q}_{s-1}^{-}.

We distinguish two cases:

\displaystyle(\bm{C}1)_{s-1}:\ {\rm dist}(\tilde{Q}_{s-1}^{-},Q_{s-1}^{+})>100N_{s}^{10}

(4.4)

and

\displaystyle(\bm{C}2)_{s-1}:\ {\rm dist}(\tilde{Q}_{s-1}^{-},Q_{s-1}^{+})\leq 100N_{s}^{10}.

(4.5)

Let

\displaystyle\mathbb{Z}^{d}\ni\bm{l}_{s-1}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lc}\bm{0},&\text{if \eqref{c1} holds true},\\
\bm{i}_{s-1}-\bm{j}_{s-1},&\text{if \eqref{c2} holds true},\end{array}\right.

where
\bm{i}_{s-1}\in Q_{s-1}^{+}
and
\bm{j}_{s-1}\in\tilde{Q}_{s-1}^{-}
such that
\|\bm{i}_{s-1}-\bm{j}_{s-1}\|\leq 100N_{s}^{10}
in
(\bm{C}2)_{s-1}
. Set
\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{0}=\{\bm{k}\}\ (\bm{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d})
. Let
\Lambda\subset\mathbb{Z}^{d}
be a finite set. We say
\Lambda
is
(s-1)
-
{\rm good}
iff

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\bm{k}^{\prime}\in Q_{s^{\prime}},\ \tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}^{\prime}}^{s^{\prime}}\subset\Lambda,\ \tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}^{\prime}}^{s^{\prime}}\subset\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{s^{\prime}+1}\Rightarrow\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s^{\prime}+1}\subset\Lambda,\ \text{for}\ s^{\prime}<s-1,\\
\{\bm{k}\in P_{s-1}:\ \tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s-1}\subset\Lambda\}\cap Q_{s-1}=\emptyset.\end{array}\right.

Then we have

(\bm{a})_{s}:
There is
P_{s}\subset\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}^{d}
so that the following holds true. In the case of
(\bm{C}1)_{s-1}
, we have

\displaystyle P_{s}=Q_{s-1}\subset\left\{\bm{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=0}^{s-1}\bm{l}_{i}:\ \min_{\sigma=\pm 1}\|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}+\sigma\theta_{s-1}\|_{\mathbb{T}}<\delta_{s-1}\right\}.

(4.6)

For the case of
(\bm{C}2)_{s-1}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{l}P_{s}\subset\left\{\bm{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}+\frac{1}{2}\sum\limits_{i=0}^{s-1}\bm{l}_{i}:\ \|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}\|_{\mathbb{T}}<3\delta_{s-1}^{\frac{1}{100}}\right\},\\
\text{or}\ P_{s}\subset\left\{\bm{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}+\frac{1}{2}\sum\limits_{i=0}^{s-1}\bm{l}_{i}:\ \|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}+\frac{1}{2}\|_{\mathbb{T}}<3\delta_{s-1}^{\frac{1}{100}}\right\}.\end{array}

For every
\bm{k}\in P_{s}
, we can find resonant blocks
\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{s},2\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{s}\subset\mathbb{Z}^{d}
and the enlarged resonant block
\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s}\subset\mathbb{Z}^{d}
with the following properties. If (
4.4
) holds true, then

\displaystyle\Lambda_{N_{s}}(\bm{k})\subset\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{s}\subset\Lambda_{N_{s}+50N_{s-1}^{100}}(\bm{k}),

\displaystyle\Lambda_{2N_{s}}(\bm{k})\subset 2\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{s}\subset\Lambda_{2N_{s}+50N_{s-1}^{100}}(\bm{k}),

\displaystyle\Lambda_{N_{s}^{10}}(\bm{k})\subset\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s}\subset\Lambda_{N_{s}^{10}+50N_{s-1}^{100}}(\bm{k}),

and if (
4.5
) holds true, then

\displaystyle\Lambda_{100N_{s}^{10}}(\bm{k})\subset\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{s}\subset\Lambda_{100N_{s}^{10}+50N_{s-1}^{100}}(\bm{k}),

\displaystyle\Lambda_{200N_{s}^{10}}(\bm{k})\subset 2\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{s}\subset\Lambda_{200N_{s}^{10}+50N_{s-1}^{100}}(\bm{k}),

\displaystyle\Lambda_{N_{s}^{100}}(\bm{k})\subset\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s}\subset\Lambda_{N_{s}^{100}+50N_{s-1}^{100}}(\bm{k}).

These resonant blocks are constructed to satisfy the following two properties:

(\bm{a}1)_{s}
:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{s}\cap\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}^{\prime}}^{s^{\prime}}\neq\emptyset\ (s^{\prime}<s)\Rightarrow\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}^{\prime}}^{s^{\prime}}\subset\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{s},\\
2\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{s}\cap\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}^{\prime}}^{s^{\prime}}\neq\emptyset\ (s^{\prime}<s)\Rightarrow\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}^{\prime}}^{s^{\prime}}\subset 2\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{s},\\
\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s}\cap\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}^{\prime}}^{s^{\prime}}\neq\emptyset\ (s^{\prime}<s)\Rightarrow\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}^{\prime}}^{s^{\prime}}\subset\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s},\\
{\rm dist}(\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s},\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}^{\prime}}^{s})>10{\rm diam}(\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s})\ \text{for}\ \bm{k}\neq\bm{k}^{\prime}\in P_{s}.\end{array}\right.

(\bm{a}2)_{s}
: The translation of
\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s}

\displaystyle\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s}-\bm{k}\subset\mathbb{Z}^{d}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=0}^{s-1}\bm{l}_{i},

is independent of
\bm{k}\in P_{s}
and symmetrical about the origin.

We denote

\displaystyle\zeta_{s}={\rm diam}(\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{s}),\ \tilde{\zeta}_{s}={\rm diam}(\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s}).

(4.13)

(\bm{b})_{s}
:
Q_{s-1}
is covered by
\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{s}\ (\bm{k}\in P_{s})
in the sense that for every
\bm{k}^{\prime}\in Q_{s-1}
, there exists a
\bm{k}\in P_{s}
such that

\displaystyle\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}^{\prime}}^{s-1}\subset\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{s}.

(4.14)

(\bm{c})_{s}
: For each
\bm{k}\in P_{s}
,
\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s}
contains a subset
A_{\bm{k}}^{s}\subset\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{s}
with
\#A_{\bm{k}}^{s}\leq 2^{s}
such that
\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s}\setminus A_{\bm{k}}^{s}
is
(s-1)
-
{\rm good}
. Moreover,
A_{\bm{k}}^{s}-\bm{k}
is independent of
\bm{k}
and is symmetrical about the origin.

(\bm{d})_{s}
: There is a
\theta_{s}=\theta_{s}(E)\in\mathbb{C}
with the following properties. Let
\bm{k}\in P_{s}
. Replacing
\theta+\bm{n}\cdot\bm{\omega}
by
z+(\bm{n}-\bm{k})\cdot\bm{\omega}
for
\bm{n}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
and restricting
z
in

\displaystyle\left\{z\in\mathbb{C}:\ \min_{\sigma=\pm 1}\|z+\sigma\theta_{s}\|_{\mathbb{T}}<\delta_{s}^{\frac{1}{10^{4}}}\right\},

(4.15)

Remark 4.1
.

For notational simplicity, we have made the following simplification: for given
\Lambda\subset\mathbb{Z}^{d}
,
\bm{k}\in\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}^{d}
and
\mathcal{A}(z):\ell^{2}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})\rightarrow\ell^{2}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
, we define
(\mathcal{A}(z))_{\Lambda-\bm{k}}
as

\displaystyle(\mathcal{A}(z))_{\Lambda-\bm{k}}(\bm{i},\bm{j})=(\mathcal{A}(z-\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}))_{\Lambda}(\bm{i}+\bm{k},\bm{j}+\bm{k})\text{ for all $\bm{i},\bm{j}\in\Lambda-\bm{k}$},

where
\bm{i}+\bm{k},\bm{j}+\bm{k}\in\Lambda\subset\mathbb{Z}^{d}
.

We write

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{s}(z)=(\mathcal{T}(z))_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s}-\bm{k}}=((v(z+\bm{n}\cdot\bm{\omega})-E)\delta_{\bm{n},\bm{n}^{\prime}}+\varepsilon\mathcal{W})_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s}-\bm{k}}.

Then
(\mathcal{M}_{s}(z))_{(\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s}\setminus A_{\bm{k}}^{s})-\bm{k}}
is invertible and we can define the Schur complement

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{s}(z)

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{M}_{s}(z))_{A_{\bm{k}}^{s}-\bm{k}}-\left(\mathcal{R}_{A_{\bm{k}}^{s}-\bm{k}}\mathcal{M}_{s}(z)\mathcal{R}_{(\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s}\setminus A_{\bm{k}}^{s})-\bm{k}}((\mathcal{M}_{s}(z))_{(\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s}\setminus A_{\bm{k}}^{s})-\bm{k}})^{-1}\right.

\displaystyle\ \ \left.\times\ \mathcal{R}_{(\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s}\setminus A_{\bm{k}}^{s})-\bm{k}}\mathcal{M}_{s}(z)\mathcal{R}_{A_{\bm{k}}^{s}-\bm{k}}\right).

Moreover, if
z
belongs to the set in (
4.15
), then we have

\displaystyle\max_{\bm{x}\in A_{\bm{k}}^{s}-\bm{k}}\sum_{\bm{y}\in A_{\bm{k}}^{s}-\bm{k}}|\mathcal{S}_{s}(z)(\bm{x},\bm{y})|<2|v|_{R}+\sum_{l=0}^{s-1}\delta_{l}<4|v|_{R}

(4.16)

and

\displaystyle\left|\det\mathcal{S}_{s}(z)\right|\geq\delta_{s-1}\|z-\theta_{s}\|_{\mathbb{T}}\cdot\|z+\theta_{s}\|_{\mathbb{T}}.

(4.17)

Combining the Schur complement lemma (cf. Lemma
A.1
), we get

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s}}^{-1}\|

\displaystyle<\delta_{s-1}^{-2}\|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}-\theta_{s}\|_{\mathbb{T}}^{-1}\cdot\|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}+\theta_{s}\|_{\mathbb{T}}^{-1}.

(4.18)

(\bm{e})_{s}
: Let

\displaystyle Q_{s}^{\pm}

\displaystyle=\{\bm{k}\in P_{s}:\ \|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}\pm\theta_{s}\|_{\mathbb{T}}<\delta_{s}\},\ Q_{s}=Q_{s}^{+}\cup Q_{s}^{-},

\displaystyle\tilde{Q}_{s}^{\pm}

\displaystyle=\{\bm{k}\in P_{s}:\ \|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}\pm\theta_{s}\|_{\mathbb{T}}<\delta_{s}^{\frac{1}{100}}\},\ \tilde{Q}_{s}=\tilde{Q}_{s}^{+}\cup\tilde{Q}_{s}^{-},

and

\displaystyle\alpha_{0}=\frac{3}{4}\alpha,\ \alpha_{s}=\alpha_{s-1}\left(1-\frac{50\times 10^{5\rho^{\prime}}}{\alpha\log^{\rho-\rho^{\prime}}N_{s}}\right).

(4.19)

Thus,

\displaystyle\alpha_{s}\searrow\alpha_{\infty}\geq\frac{\alpha}{2}.

If
\bm{k}\in P_{s}\setminus Q_{s}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s}}^{-1}\|\leq\delta_{s-1}^{-2}\delta_{s}^{-2}<\delta_{s}^{-3},

(4.20)

\displaystyle|\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s}}^{-1}(\bm{x},\bm{y})|<e^{-\alpha^{\prime}_{s-1}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|)}\text{ for $\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|>\frac{\tilde{\zeta}_{s}}{10}$},

(4.21)

where

\displaystyle\alpha^{\prime}_{s-1}=\alpha_{s-1}\left(1-\frac{20\times 10^{5\rho^{\prime}}}{\alpha\log^{\rho-\rho^{\prime}}N_{s}}\right)>\alpha_{s}.

(4.22)

We say a finite set
\Lambda\subset\mathbb{Z}^{d}
is
s
-
{\rm good}
iff

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\bm{k}^{\prime}\in Q_{s^{\prime}},\ \tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}^{\prime}}^{s^{\prime}}\subset\Lambda,\ \tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}^{\prime}}^{s^{\prime}}\subset\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{s^{\prime}+1}\Rightarrow\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s^{\prime}+1}\subset\Lambda\ \text{for}\ s^{\prime}<s,\\
\{\bm{k}\in P_{s}:\ \tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s}\subset\Lambda\}\cap Q_{s}=\emptyset.\end{array}\right.

Assume that
\Lambda
is
s
-
{\rm good}
. Then

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}\|\leq 2\delta_{s-1}^{-3}\sup_{\{\bm{k}\in P_{s}:\ \tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s}\subset\Lambda\}}\left(\|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}-\theta_{s}\|_{\mathbb{T}}^{-1}\cdot\|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}+\theta_{s}\|_{\mathbb{T}}^{-1}\right)<\delta_{s}^{-3},

(4.23)

\displaystyle|\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(\bm{x},\bm{y})|<e^{-\alpha_{s}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|)}\text{ for $\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|>10\tilde{\zeta}_{s}$}.

(4.24)

(\bm{f})_{s}
: We have

\displaystyle\left\{\bm{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=0}^{s-1}\bm{l}_{i}:\ \min_{\sigma=\pm 1}\|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}+\sigma\theta_{s}\|_{\mathbb{T}}<10\delta_{s}^{\frac{1}{100}}\right\}\subset P_{s}.

(4.25)

The main theorem of this section is

Theorem 4.2
.

Let
\bm{\omega}\in DC_{\tau,\gamma}
. Then there is some
\varepsilon_{0}=\varepsilon_{0}(d,\tau,\gamma,v,R,\alpha,\rho,\rho^{\prime})>0
so that for
0<|\varepsilon|<\varepsilon_{0}
, the statement
\mathscr{P}_{s}
holds true for all
s\geq 1
.

The following three subsections are devoted to proving Theorem
4.2
.

4.1.
The initial step

Recalling
v(\theta_{0})=E
,
\mathcal{D}_{\bm{n}}=v(\theta+\bm{n}\cdot\bm{\omega})-E
and (
1.3
), we have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{D}_{\bm{n}}|=|v(\theta+\bm{n}\cdot\bm{\omega})-v(\theta_{0})|\geq\kappa_{1}\|\theta+\bm{n}\cdot\bm{\omega}+\theta_{0}\|_{\mathbb{T}}\cdot\|\theta+\bm{n}\cdot\bm{\omega}-\theta_{0}\|_{\mathbb{T}}.

Denote

\displaystyle P_{0}=\mathbb{Z}^{d},\ Q_{0}=\{\bm{k}\in P_{0}:\ \min(\|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}+\theta_{0}\|_{\mathbb{T}},\|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}-\theta_{0}\|_{\mathbb{T}})<\delta_{0}\}.

We say a finite set
\Lambda\subset\mathbb{Z}^{d}
is
0
-
{\rm good}
iff
\Lambda\cap Q_{0}=\emptyset
. Then we have

Lemma 4.3
.

If the set
\Lambda\subset\mathbb{Z}^{d}
is
0
-
{\rm good}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}\|\leq 2\kappa_{1}^{-1}\delta_{0}^{-2},

(4.26)

and

\displaystyle|\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(\bm{x},\bm{y})|\leq e^{-\frac{3}{4}\alpha\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|)}\ \text{for $\bm{x}\neq\bm{y}$}.

(4.27)

Proof.

Since
\Lambda
is
0
-good, for
\bm{n}\in\Lambda
, we have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{D}_{\bm{n}}|\geq\kappa_{1}\|\theta+\bm{n}\cdot\bm{\omega}+\theta_{0}\|_{\mathbb{T}}\cdot\|\theta+\bm{n}\cdot\bm{\omega}-\theta_{0}\|_{\mathbb{T}}\geq\kappa_{1}\delta_{0}^{2}.

Then

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda}^{-1}\|\leq\kappa_{1}^{-1}\delta_{0}^{-2},

(4.28)

which implies (since
|\varepsilon|<\varepsilon_{0}
and
\varepsilon_{0}=\delta_{0}^{10}
)

\displaystyle\|\varepsilon\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda}^{-1}\mathcal{W}_{\Lambda}\|\leq|\varepsilon|\cdot\|\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda}^{-1}\|\cdot\|\mathcal{W}_{\Lambda}\|<\frac{1}{2}.

Thus

\displaystyle(\mathcal{I}_{\Lambda}+\varepsilon\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda}^{-1}\mathcal{W}_{\Lambda})^{-1}=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}(-\varepsilon\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda}^{-1}\mathcal{W}_{\Lambda})^{i}

exist and

\displaystyle\|(\mathcal{I}_{\Lambda}+\varepsilon\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda}^{-1}\mathcal{W}_{\Lambda})^{-1}\|\leq\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}|(|\varepsilon|\cdot\|\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda}^{-1}\|\cdot\|\mathcal{W}_{\Lambda}\|)^{i}<2.

At this time, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}=(\mathcal{I}_{\Lambda}+\varepsilon\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda}^{-1}\mathcal{W}_{\Lambda})^{-1}\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda}^{-1}

exist and

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}\|\leq\|(\mathcal{I}_{\Lambda}+\varepsilon\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda}^{-1}\mathcal{W}_{\Lambda})^{-1}\|\cdot\|\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda}^{-1}\|\leq 2\kappa_{1}^{-1}\delta_{0}^{-2}.

Moreover, if
\bm{x}\neq\bm{y}
, we learn that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(\bm{x},\bm{y})|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}|\varepsilon|^{i}\cdot|((\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda}^{-1}\mathcal{W}_{\Lambda})^{i}\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda}^{-1})(\bm{x},\bm{y})|

\displaystyle\leq|\varepsilon|\cdot\|\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda}^{-1}\|^{2}\cdot|\mathcal{W}_{\Lambda}(\bm{x},\bm{y})|

\displaystyle\ +\left(\sum_{i=2}^{\infty}|\varepsilon|^{i}\cdot\|\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda}^{-1}\|^{i+1}\cdot|\mathcal{W}_{\Lambda}^{i}(\bm{x},\bm{y})|\right).

(4.29)

Next, we will control
|\mathcal{W}_{\Lambda}^{i}(\bm{x},\bm{y})|
for
i\geq 2
. From (
1.2
) and (
3.1
), we have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{W}_{\Lambda}^{i}(\bm{x},\bm{y})|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\bm{k}_{1},\bm{k}_{2},\cdots,\bm{k}_{i-1}\in\Lambda}|\mathcal{W}_{\Lambda}(\bm{x},\bm{k}_{1})|\cdot|\mathcal{W}_{\Lambda}(\bm{k}_{1},\bm{k}_{2})|\cdots|\mathcal{W}_{\Lambda}(\bm{k}_{i-1},\bm{y})|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\bm{k}_{1},\bm{k}_{2},\cdots,\bm{k}_{i-1}\in\Lambda}e^{-\alpha\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{k}_{1}\|)}\cdot e^{-\alpha\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{k}_{1}-\bm{k}_{2}\|)}\cdots e^{-\alpha\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{k}_{i-1}-\bm{y}\|)}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\bm{k}_{1},\bm{k}_{2},\cdots,\bm{k}_{i-1}\in\Lambda}\left(\vphantom{e^{-\frac{\alpha}{4}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{k}_{1}\|)}}e^{-\frac{\alpha}{4}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{k}_{1}\|)}\cdot e^{-\frac{\alpha}{4}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{k}_{1}-\bm{k}_{2}\|)}\cdots e^{-\frac{\alpha}{4}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{k}_{i-1}-\bm{y}\|)}\right.

\displaystyle\ \ \left.\vphantom{e^{-\frac{\alpha}{4}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{k}_{1}\|)}}\times e^{-\frac{3}{4}\alpha\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{k}_{1}\|)}\cdot e^{-\frac{3}{4}\alpha\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{k}_{1}-\bm{k}_{2}\|)}\cdots e^{-\frac{3}{4}\alpha\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{k}_{i-1}-\bm{y}\|)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(D\left(\frac{\alpha}{4}\right)\right)^{i-1}e^{-\frac{3}{4}\alpha\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|)+\frac{3}{4}\alpha C(\rho)\log^{\rho}},

(4.30)

where
e^{-\frac{\alpha}{4}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{k}_{i-1}-\bm{y}\|)}\leq 1
and (
2.1
) are used.
Since
|\varepsilon|<\varepsilon_{0}
,
\varepsilon_{0}=\delta_{0}^{10}
, (
1.2
), (
4.28
) and (
4.30
), we obtain

\displaystyle|\varepsilon|\cdot\|\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda}^{-1}\|^{2}\cdot|\mathcal{W}_{\Lambda}(\bm{x},\bm{y})|\leq\frac{1}{2}e^{-\alpha\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|)},

(4.31)

and

\displaystyle\ \ \sum_{i=2}^{\infty}|\varepsilon|^{i}\cdot\|\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda}^{-1}\|^{i+1}\cdot|\mathcal{W}_{\Lambda}^{i}(\bm{x},\bm{y})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \ \left(\sum_{i=2}^{\infty}|\varepsilon|^{i}\cdot\|\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda}^{-1}\|^{i+1}\cdot\left(D\left(\frac{\alpha}{4}\right)\right)^{i-1}e^{\frac{3}{4}\alpha C(\rho)\log^{\rho}i}\right)e^{-\frac{3}{4}\alpha\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \ \frac{1}{2}e^{-\frac{3}{4}\alpha\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|)}.

(4.32)

Combining (
4.29
), (
4.31
) and (
4.32
) gives

\displaystyle|\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(\bm{x},\bm{y})|\leq e^{-\frac{3}{4}\alpha\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|)}\ \text{for $\bm{x}\neq\bm{y}$}.

We complete this proof.
∎

Remark 4.2
.

If we directly obtain

\displaystyle\ \ \sum_{\bm{k}_{1},\bm{k}_{2},\cdots,\bm{k}_{i-1}\in\Lambda}|\mathcal{W}_{\Lambda}(\bm{x},\bm{k}_{1})|\cdot|\mathcal{W}_{\Lambda}(\bm{k}_{1},\bm{k}_{2})|\cdots|\mathcal{W}_{\Lambda}(\bm{k}_{i-1},\bm{y})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \ \sum_{\bm{k}_{1},\bm{k}_{2},\cdots,\bm{k}_{i-1}\in\Lambda}e^{-\alpha\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|)+\alpha C(\rho)\log^{\rho}i}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \ (\#\Lambda)^{i-1}e^{-\alpha\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|)+\alpha C(\rho)\log^{\rho}i}

in (
4.30
) by using (
1.2
) and (
3.1
), the relevant series in (
4.32
) will diverge when
\#\Lambda
is sufficiently large. In practice, we utilize the partial decay of
\mathcal{W}
to overcome this difficulty, and this technique will be widely applied in subsequent sections. The loss term
\frac{\alpha}{4}
is not essential and can be replaced by any constant
c\in(0,\alpha)
.

Based on Lemma
4.3
, we derive the following frequently used corollary:

Corollary 4.4
.

Let
\Lambda^{\prime},\Lambda\subset\mathbb{Z}^{d}
be finite sets. If
\Lambda^{\prime}\subset\Lambda
is
0
-
good
,
\bm{u}\in\Lambda^{\prime}
and
\bm{v}\in\Lambda\setminus\Lambda^{\prime}
, then there is some
\bm{u}^{\prime}\in\Lambda\setminus\Lambda^{\prime}
such that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(\bm{u},\bm{v})|\leq(\#\Lambda^{\prime})\cdot e^{-\frac{3}{4}\alpha\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{u}-\bm{u}^{\prime}\|)}\cdot|\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(\bm{u}^{\prime},\bm{v})|\cdot\delta_{0}^{-3}.

(4.33)

Morover, if
\Lambda^{\prime}=\Lambda_{\frac{1}{2}N_{1}}(\bm{u})\cap\Lambda
is
0
-
good
, then

\displaystyle|\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(\bm{u},\bm{v})|\leq e^{-\frac{3}{4}\alpha\left(1-\frac{6\times 10^{5\rho^{\prime}}}{\alpha\log^{\rho-\rho^{\prime}}N_{1}}\right)\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{u}-\bm{u}^{\prime}\|)}\cdot|\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(\bm{u}^{\prime},\bm{v})|.

(4.34)

Remark 4.3
.

If
\bm{v}\in\Lambda^{\prime}
and
\bm{u}\in\Lambda\setminus\Lambda^{\prime}
, we have a similar argument: there is some
\bm{v}^{\prime}\in\Lambda\setminus\Lambda^{\prime}
such that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(\bm{u},\bm{v})|\leq(\#\Lambda^{\prime})\cdot e^{-\frac{3}{4}\alpha\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{v}-\bm{v}^{\prime}\|)}\cdot|\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(\bm{u},\bm{v}^{\prime})|\cdot\delta_{0}^{-3}.

(4.35)

Proof.

Using the resolvent identity implies

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(\bm{u},\bm{v})=-\varepsilon\sum_{\bm{w}\in\Lambda^{\prime}\atop\bm{w}^{\prime}\in\Lambda\setminus\Lambda^{\prime}}\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda^{\prime}}^{-1}(\bm{u},\bm{w})\cdot\mathcal{W}(\bm{w},\bm{w}^{\prime})\cdot\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(\bm{w}^{\prime},\bm{v}),

and then

\displaystyle|\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(\bm{u},\bm{v})|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\bm{w}\in\Lambda^{\prime}\atop\bm{w}^{\prime}\in\Lambda\setminus\Lambda^{\prime}}\left|\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda^{\prime}}^{-1}(\bm{u},\bm{w})\cdot\mathcal{W}(\bm{w},\bm{w}^{\prime})\cdot e^{\frac{\alpha}{10}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{w}-\bm{w}^{\prime}\|)}\cdot\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(\bm{w}^{\prime},\bm{v})\right|

\displaystyle\ \ \times\left(\sum_{\bm{w}\in\Lambda^{\prime}\atop\bm{w}^{\prime}\in\Lambda\setminus\Lambda^{\prime}}e^{-\frac{\alpha}{10}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{w}-\bm{w}^{\prime}\|)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\bm{w}\in\Lambda^{\prime}\atop\bm{w}^{\prime}\in\Lambda\setminus\Lambda^{\prime}}\left|\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda^{\prime}}^{-1}(\bm{u},\bm{w})\cdot\mathcal{W}(\bm{w},\bm{w}^{\prime})\cdot e^{\frac{\alpha}{10}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{w}-\bm{w}^{\prime}\|)}\cdot\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(\bm{w}^{\prime},\bm{v})\right|

\displaystyle\ \ \times(\#\Lambda^{\prime})\cdot D\left(\frac{\alpha}{10}\right),

(4.36)

where
D\left(\frac{\alpha}{10}\right)
is defined in (
2.1
). Since
\Lambda^{\prime}
and
\Lambda\setminus\Lambda^{\prime}
are finite sets, there are
\bm{u}^{*}\in\Lambda^{\prime}
and
\bm{u}^{\prime}\in\Lambda\setminus\Lambda^{\prime}
such that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda^{\prime}}^{-1}(\bm{u},\bm{u}^{*})\cdot\mathcal{W}(\bm{u}^{*},\bm{u}^{\prime})\cdot e^{\frac{\alpha}{10}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{u}^{*}-\bm{u}^{\prime}\|)}\cdot\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(\bm{u}^{\prime},\bm{v})|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \ \max_{\bm{w}\in\Lambda^{\prime}\atop\bm{w}^{\prime}\in\Lambda\setminus\Lambda^{\prime}}|\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda^{\prime}}^{-1}(\bm{u},\bm{w})\cdot\mathcal{W}(\bm{w},\bm{w}^{\prime})\cdot e^{\frac{\alpha}{10}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{w}-\bm{w}^{\prime}\|)}\cdot\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(\bm{w}^{\prime},\bm{v})|.

(4.37)

If
\bm{u}^{*}=\bm{u}
, from
\Lambda^{\prime}
is
0
-good (cf. (
4.26
)) and (
1.2
), we can get

\displaystyle\ \ |\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda^{\prime}}^{-1}(\bm{u},\bm{u}^{*})\cdot\mathcal{W}(\bm{u}^{*},\bm{u}^{\prime})\cdot e^{\frac{\alpha}{10}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{u}^{*}-\bm{u}^{\prime}\|)}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \ 2\kappa_{1}^{-1}\delta_{0}^{-2}\cdot e^{-\frac{9}{10}\alpha\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{u}-\bm{u}^{\prime}\|)}.

(4.38)

If
\bm{u}^{*}\neq\bm{u}
, since
\Lambda^{\prime}
is
0
-good (cf. (
4.27
)), (
1.2
) and (
3.1
), we have

\displaystyle\ \ |\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda^{\prime}}^{-1}(\bm{u},\bm{u}^{*})\cdot\mathcal{W}(\bm{u}^{*},\bm{u}^{\prime})\cdot e^{\frac{\alpha}{10}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{u}^{*}-\bm{u}^{\prime}\|)}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \ 2e^{-\frac{3}{4}\alpha\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{u}-\bm{u}^{*}\|)}\cdot e^{-\frac{9}{10}\alpha\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{u}^{*}-\bm{u}^{\prime}\|)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \ 2e^{-\frac{3}{4}\alpha\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{u}-\bm{u}^{\prime}\|)+\frac{3}{4}\alpha C(\rho)\log^{\rho}2}.

(4.39)

Combining (
4.38
), (
4.39
) and
\delta_{0}\ll 1
gives

\displaystyle\ \ |\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda^{\prime}}^{-1}(\bm{u},\bm{u}^{*})\cdot\mathcal{W}(\bm{u}^{*},\bm{u}^{\prime})\cdot e^{\frac{\alpha}{10}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{u}^{*}-\bm{u}^{\prime}\|)}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \ 2\kappa_{1}^{-1}\delta_{0}^{-2}\cdot e^{-\frac{3}{4}\alpha\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{u}-\bm{u}^{\prime}\|)}.

(4.40)

By (
4.36
), (
4.37
), (
4.40
) and
\delta_{0}\ll 1
, we obtain

\displaystyle|\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(\bm{u},\bm{v})|\leq(\#\Lambda^{\prime})\cdot e^{-\frac{3}{4}\alpha\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{u}-\bm{u}^{\prime}\|)}\cdot|\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(\bm{u}^{\prime},\bm{v})|\cdot\delta_{0}^{-3}.

(4.41)

Morover, if
\Lambda^{\prime}=\Lambda_{\frac{1}{2}N_{1}}(\bm{u})\cap\Lambda
is 0-good,
\|\bm{u}-\bm{u}^{\prime}\|\geq\frac{1}{2}N_{1}
(since
\bm{u}^{\prime}\in\Lambda\setminus\Lambda^{\prime}
) and
(\#\Lambda^{\prime})\leq(N_{1}+1)^{d}
. Therefore, by (
4.41
), (
4.3
) and
N_{1}\gg 1
, we have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(\bm{u},\bm{v})|\leq e^{-\frac{3}{4}\alpha\left(1-\frac{6\times 10^{5\rho^{\prime}}}{\alpha\log^{\rho-\rho^{\prime}}N_{1}}\right)\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{u}-\bm{u}^{\prime}\|)}\cdot|\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(\bm{u}^{\prime},\bm{v})|.

We finish this proof.
∎

4.2.
Verification of
\mathscr{P}_{1}

If
\Lambda\cap Q_{0}\neq\emptyset
, the Neumann series argument from the previous subsection fails. We therefore estimate
\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}
using the resolvent identity argument whenever
\Lambda
is
1
-
{\rm good}
.

Recall that

\displaystyle N_{1}=\left[e^{\left|\log\delta_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{\rho^{\prime}}}}\right]

and define (cf. (
4.1
))

\displaystyle Q_{0}^{\pm}

\displaystyle=\left\{\bm{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}:\ \|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}\pm\theta_{0}\|_{\mathbb{T}}<\delta_{0}\right\},\ Q_{0}=Q_{0}^{+}\cup Q_{0}^{-},

\displaystyle\tilde{Q}_{0}^{\pm}

\displaystyle=\left\{\bm{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}:\ \|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}\pm\theta_{0}\|_{\mathbb{T}}<\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{100}}\right\},\ \tilde{Q}_{0}=\tilde{Q}_{0}^{+}\cup\tilde{Q}_{0}^{-}.

We distinguish the verification into three steps.

Step 1

:
Estimates of
\|\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}}^{-1}\|
.

In this step, we will find
\theta_{1}=\theta_{1}(E)
so that

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}}^{-1}\|

\displaystyle<\delta_{0}^{-2}\|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}-\theta_{1}\|_{\mathbb{T}}^{-1}\cdot\|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}+\theta_{1}\|_{\mathbb{T}}^{-1}.

We again divide the discussions into two cases.

Case 1

: The case
(\bm{C}1)_{0}
occurs, i.e.,

\displaystyle\text{dist}\left(\tilde{Q}_{0}^{-},Q_{0}^{+}\right)>100N_{1}^{10}.

(4.42)

Remark 4.4
.

We have in fact

\displaystyle\text{dist}\left(\tilde{Q}_{0}^{-},Q_{0}^{+}\right)=\text{dist}\left(\tilde{Q}_{0}^{+},Q_{0}^{-}\right).

Thus (
4.42
) also implies

\displaystyle\text{dist}\left(\tilde{Q}_{0}^{+},Q_{0}^{-}\right)>100N_{1}^{10}.

We refer to
[
CSZ24b
]
for a detailed proof.

Assuming (
4.42
) holds true, we define

\displaystyle P_{1}=Q_{0}=\{\bm{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}:\ \min(\|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}+\theta_{0}\|_{\mathbb{T}},\|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}-\theta_{0}\|_{\mathbb{T}})<\delta_{0}\}.

(4.43)

Associate each
\bm{k}\in P_{1}
with
\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{1}:=\Lambda_{N_{1}}(\bm{k})
,
2\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{1}=\Lambda_{2N_{1}}(\bm{k})
and
\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}:=\Lambda_{N_{1}^{10}}(\bm{k})
. Then
\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}-\bm{k}\subset\mathbb{Z}^{d}
is independent of
\bm{k}\in P_{1}
and symmetrical about the origin. If
\bm{k}\neq\bm{k}^{\prime}\in P_{1}
, then

\displaystyle\|\bm{k}-\bm{k}^{\prime}\|\geq\min\left(100N_{1}^{10},\left(\frac{\gamma}{2\delta_{0}}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tau}}\right)\geq 100N_{1}^{10}.

Thus

\displaystyle\text{dist}\left(\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1},\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}^{\prime}}^{1}\right)>10\tilde{\zeta}_{1}\ \text{for}\ \bm{k}\neq\bm{k}^{\prime}\in P_{1}.

For
\bm{k}\in Q_{0}^{-}
, we consider

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{1}(z):=(\mathcal{T}(z))_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}-\bm{k}}=\left((v(z+\bm{n}\cdot\bm{\omega})-E)\delta_{\bm{n},\bm{n}^{\prime}}+\varepsilon\mathcal{W}\right)_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}-\bm{k}}

defined in

\displaystyle\left\{z\in\mathbb{C}:\ |z-\theta_{0}|\leq\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{10}}\right\}.

(4.44)

For
\bm{n}\in\left(\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}-\bm{k}\right)\setminus\{\bm{0}\}
, we have for
0<\delta_{0}\ll 1
,

\displaystyle\|z+\bm{n}\cdot\bm{\omega}-\theta_{0}\|_{\mathbb{T}}

\displaystyle\geq\|\bm{n}\cdot\bm{\omega}\|_{\mathbb{T}}-|z-\theta_{0}|

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\gamma}{(N_{1}^{10})^{\tau}}-\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{10}}

\displaystyle>\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{10^{4}}}\ \text{(since \eqref{indpa})}.

For
\bm{n}\in\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}-\bm{k}
, we have (since
{\rm dist}(\bm{k},\tilde{Q}_{0}^{+})>100N_{1}^{10}
and
\bm{k}\in Q_{0}^{-}
)

\displaystyle\|z+\bm{n}\cdot\bm{\omega}+\theta_{0}\|_{\mathbb{T}}

\displaystyle\geq\|\theta+(\bm{n}+\bm{k})\cdot\bm{\omega}+\theta_{0}\|_{\mathbb{T}}-|z-\theta_{0}|-\|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}-\theta_{0}\|_{\mathbb{T}}

\displaystyle\geq\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{100}}-\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{10}}-\delta_{0}>\frac{1}{2}\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{100}}.

Hence for
\bm{n}\in\left(\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}-\bm{k}\right)\setminus\{\bm{0}\}
,

\displaystyle|v(z+\bm{n}\cdot\bm{\omega})-E|\geq\frac{\kappa_{1}}{2}\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{100}+\frac{1}{10^{4}}}>\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{50}}\gg\varepsilon.

By Neumann series argument, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\left((\mathcal{M}_{1}(z))_{(\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}-\bm{k})\setminus\{\bm{0}\}}\right)^{-1}\right\|\leq 2\delta_{0}^{-\frac{1}{50}}.

(4.45)

We now apply the Schur complement lemma to derive the desired estimates. According to Lemma
A.1
, the inverse
(\mathcal{M}1(z))^{-1}
is governed by the Schur complement (of
(\tilde{\Omega}{\bm{k}}^{1}-\bm{k})\setminus{\bm{0}}
):

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{1}(z)

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{M}_{1}(z))_{\{\bm{0}\}}-\left(\mathcal{R}_{\{\bm{0}\}}\mathcal{M}_{1}(z)\mathcal{R}_{(\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}-\bm{k})\setminus\{\bm{0}\}}\left((\mathcal{M}_{1}(z))_{(\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}-\bm{k})\setminus\{\bm{0}\}}\right)^{-1}\right.

\displaystyle\ \ \left.\vphantom{\left((\mathcal{M}_{1}(z))_{(\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}-\bm{k})\setminus\{\bm{0}\}}\right)^{-1}}\times\mathcal{R}_{(\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}-\bm{k})\setminus\{\bm{0}\}}\mathcal{M}_{1}(z)\mathcal{R}_{\{\bm{0}\}}\right)

\displaystyle=v(z)-E+r(z)=g(z)((z-\theta_{0})+r_{1}(z)),

where
g(z)
and
r_{1}(z)
are analytic functions in the domain specified by (
4.44
). These functions satisfy the bounds:
|g(z)|>\kappa_{1}\|z+\theta_{0}\|_{\mathbb{T}}>\frac{\kappa_{1}}{2}\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{100}}
and
|r_{1}(z)|\leq\varepsilon^{2}D^{2}(\alpha)\delta_{0}^{-\frac{1}{50}}<\varepsilon
. Since

\displaystyle|r_{1}(z)|<|z-\theta_{0}|\ \text{for}\ |z-\theta_{0}|=\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{10}},

using the Rouché’s theorem implies

\displaystyle(z-\theta_{0})+r_{1}(z)=0

has a unique root
\theta_{1}
in the set of (
4.44
) satisfying

\displaystyle|\theta_{0}-\theta_{1}|=|r_{1}(\theta_{1})|<\varepsilon.

(4.46)

Since
|r_{1}(z)|<\varepsilon
and (
4.46
), we get for
|z|=\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{10}}
,

\displaystyle\frac{|r_{1}(\theta_{1})-r_{1}(z)|}{|z-\theta_{0}+r_{1}(z)|}\leq 4\varepsilon\delta_{0}^{-\frac{1}{10}},

which combined with
\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}+r_{1}(\theta_{1})=0
shows

\displaystyle\frac{|z-\theta_{1}|}{|z-\theta_{0}+r_{1}(z)|}=\frac{|z-\theta_{0}+r_{1}(\theta_{1})|}{|z-\theta_{0}+r_{1}(z)|}\in[1-4\varepsilon\delta_{0}^{-\frac{1}{10}},1+4\varepsilon\delta_{0}^{-\frac{1}{10}}].

By the maximum modulus principle, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\leq\frac{|z-\theta_{1}|}{|z-\theta_{0}+r_{1}(z)|}\leq 2.

Moreover,
\theta_{1}
is the unique root of
\det\mathcal{M}_{1}(z)=0
in the set of (
4.44
). Since
\|z+\theta_{0}\|_{\mathbb{T}}>\frac{1}{2}\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{100}}
and
|\theta_{0}-\theta_{1}|<\varepsilon
, we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\|z+\theta_{0}\|_{\mathbb{T}}\leq\|z+\theta_{1}\|_{\mathbb{T}}\leq 2\|z+\theta_{0}\|_{\mathbb{T}}.

Then by (
4.45
) and (
A.21
), we have for
z
being in the set of (
4.44
),

\displaystyle|\mathcal{S}_{1}(z)|

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\kappa_{1}}{4}\|z+\theta_{1}\|_{\mathbb{T}}\cdot\|z-\theta_{1}\|_{\mathbb{T}}\geq\delta_{0}\|z+\theta_{1}\|_{\mathbb{T}}\cdot\|z-\theta_{1}\|_{\mathbb{T}},

(4.47)

\displaystyle\|(\mathcal{M}_{1}(z))^{-1}\|

\displaystyle<4\left(1+\left\|\left(\mathcal{M}_{1}(z)_{(\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}-\bm{k})\setminus\{\bm{0}\}}\right)^{-1}\right\|\right)^{2}(1+|\mathcal{S}_{1}(z)|^{-1})

\displaystyle<\delta_{0}^{-2}\|z+\theta_{1}\|_{\mathbb{T}}^{-1}\cdot\|z-\theta_{1}\|_{\mathbb{T}}^{-1}.

(4.48)

Now, for
\bm{k}\in Q_{0}^{+}
, we consider
\mathcal{M}_{1}(z)
in the set

\displaystyle\left\{z\in\mathbb{C}:\ |z+\theta_{0}|\leq\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{10}}\right\}.

(4.49)

Applying an analogous argument demonstrates that
\det\mathcal{M}_{1}(z)=0
has a unique root
\theta_{1}^{\prime}
in the region defined by (
4.49
). We claim that
\theta_{1}+\theta_{1}^{\prime}=0
. Indeed, Lemma
A.2
establishes that
\det\mathcal{M}_{1}(z)
is an even function of
z
, which, combined with the uniqueness of the root, implies
\theta_{1}^{\prime}=-\theta_{1}
. Consequently, for
z
in (
4.49
), both (
4.47
) and (
4.48
) remain valid. Finally, we note that (
4.47
) and (
4.48
) continue to hold for

\displaystyle\left\{z\in\mathbb{C}:\ \min\limits_{\sigma=\pm 1}|z+\sigma\theta_{0}|\leq\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{10}}\right\}.

(4.50)

From (
4.43
), we conclude that
\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}
belongs to the region defined in (
4.50
). Therefore, for
\bm{k}\in P_{1}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}}^{-1}\|

\displaystyle=\|(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}))^{-1}\|

\displaystyle<\delta_{0}^{-2}\|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}+\theta_{1}\|_{\mathbb{T}}^{-1}\cdot\|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}-\theta_{1}\|_{\mathbb{T}}^{-1}.

(4.51)

Case 2

: The case
(\bm{C}2)_{0}
occurs, i.e.,

\displaystyle\text{dist}\left(\tilde{Q}_{0}^{-},Q_{0}^{+}\right)\leq 100N_{1}^{10}.

(4.52)

Then there exist
\bm{i}_{0}\in Q_{0}^{+}
and
\bm{j}_{0}\in\tilde{Q}_{0}^{-}
with
\|\bm{i}_{0}-\bm{j}_{0}\|\leq 100N_{1}^{10}
such that

\displaystyle\|\theta+\bm{i}_{0}\cdot\bm{\omega}+\theta_{0}\|_{\mathbb{T}}<\delta_{0},\ \|\theta+\bm{j}_{0}\cdot\bm{\omega}-\theta_{0}\|_{\mathbb{T}}<\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{100}}.

Set
\bm{l}_{0}=\bm{i}_{0}-\bm{j}_{0}
. Then

\displaystyle\|\bm{l}_{0}\|=\text{dist}\left(\tilde{Q}_{0}^{-},Q_{0}^{+}\right)=\text{dist}\left(\tilde{Q}_{0}^{+},Q_{0}^{-}\right).

Define

\displaystyle O_{1}=Q_{0}^{-}\cup(Q_{0}^{+}-\bm{l}_{0}).

For
\bm{k}\in Q_{0}^{+}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\theta+(\bm{k}-\bm{l}_{0})\cdot\bm{\omega}-\theta_{0}\|_{\mathbb{T}}

\displaystyle<\|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}+\theta_{0}\|_{\mathbb{T}}+\|\bm{l}_{0}\cdot\bm{\omega}+2\theta_{0}\|_{\mathbb{T}}

\displaystyle<\delta_{0}+\delta_{0}+\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{100}}<2\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{100}}.

Thus

\displaystyle O_{1}\subset\left\{\bm{o}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}:\ \|\theta+\bm{o}\cdot\bm{\omega}-\theta_{0}\|_{\mathbb{T}}<2\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{100}}\right\}.

For every
\bm{o}\in O_{1}
, define its mirror point

\displaystyle\bm{o}^{*}=\bm{o}+\bm{l}_{0}.

Next, define

\displaystyle P_{1}=\left\{\frac{1}{2}(\bm{o}+\bm{o}^{*}):\ \bm{o}\in O_{1}\right\}=\left\{\bm{o}+\frac{\bm{l}_{0}}{2}:\ \bm{o}\in O_{1}\right\}.

(4.53)

Associate each
\bm{k}\in P_{1}
with
\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{1}:=\Lambda_{100N_{1}^{10}}(\bm{k})
,
2\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{1}:=\Lambda_{200N_{1}^{10}}(\bm{k})
and
\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}:=\Lambda_{N_{1}^{100}}(\bm{k})
. Thus

\displaystyle Q_{0}\subset\bigcup_{\bm{k}\in P_{1}}\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{1}

and
\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}-\bm{k}\subset\mathbb{Z}^{d}+\frac{\bm{l}_{0}}{2}
is independent of
\bm{k}\in P_{1}
and symmetrical about origin. Notice that

\displaystyle\min

\displaystyle\left(\left\|\frac{\bm{l}_{0}}{2}\cdot\bm{\omega}+\theta_{0}\right\|_{\mathbb{T}},\left\|\frac{\bm{l}_{0}}{2}\cdot\bm{\omega}+\theta_{0}-\frac{1}{2}\right\|_{\mathbb{T}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\|\bm{l}_{0}\cdot\bm{\omega}+2\theta_{0}\|_{\mathbb{T}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}(\|\theta+\bm{i}_{0}\cdot\bm{\omega}+\theta_{0}\|_{\mathbb{T}}+\|\theta+\bm{j}_{0}\cdot\bm{\omega}-\theta_{0}\|_{\mathbb{T}})<\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{100}}.

Since
\delta_{0}\ll 1
, only one of

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\bm{l}_{0}}{2}\cdot\bm{\omega}+\theta_{0}\right\|_{\mathbb{T}}<\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{100}}\ \text{and}\ \left\|\frac{\bm{l}_{0}}{2}\cdot\bm{\omega}+\theta_{0}-\frac{1}{2}\right\|_{\mathbb{T}}<\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{100}}

holds true. First, we consider the case of

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\bm{l}_{0}}{2}\cdot\bm{\omega}+\theta_{0}\right\|_{\mathbb{T}}<\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{100}}.

(4.54)

Let
\bm{k}\in P_{1}
. Since
\bm{k}=\frac{1}{2}(\bm{o}+\bm{o}^{*})=\bm{o}+\frac{\bm{l}_{0}}{2}
(for some
\bm{o}\in O_{1}
), we have

\displaystyle\|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}\|_{\mathbb{T}}\leq\|\theta+\bm{o}\cdot\bm{\omega}-\theta_{0}\|_{\mathbb{T}}+\left\|\frac{\bm{l}_{0}}{2}\cdot\bm{\omega}+\theta_{0}\right\|_{\mathbb{T}}<3\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{100}}.

(4.55)

Thus if
\bm{k}\neq\bm{k}^{\prime}\in P_{1}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\bm{k}-\bm{k}^{\prime}\|\geq\left(\frac{\gamma}{6\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{100}}}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tau}}\gg 100N_{1}^{100},

which implies

\displaystyle\text{dist}\left(\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1},\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}^{\prime}}^{1}\right)>10\tilde{\zeta}_{1}\ \text{for}\ \bm{k}\neq\bm{k}^{\prime}\in P_{1}.

Consider

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{1}(z):=(\mathcal{T}(z))_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}-\bm{k}}=\left((v(z+\bm{n}\cdot\bm{\omega})-E)\delta_{\bm{n},\bm{n}^{\prime}}+\varepsilon\mathcal{W}\right)_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}-\bm{k}}

in the set of

\displaystyle\left\{z\in\mathbb{C}:\ |z|\leq\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{10^{3}}}\right\}.

(4.56)

For
\bm{n}\neq\pm\frac{\bm{l}_{0}}{2}
and
\bm{n}\in\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}-\bm{k}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\bm{n}\cdot\bm{\omega}\pm\theta_{0}\|_{\mathbb{T}}

\displaystyle\geq\left\|\left(\bm{n}\mp\frac{\bm{l}_{0}}{2}\right)\cdot\bm{\omega}\right\|_{\mathbb{T}}-\left\|\frac{\bm{l}_{0}}{2}\cdot\bm{\omega}+\theta_{0}\right\|_{\mathbb{T}}

\displaystyle>\frac{\gamma}{(2N_{1}^{100})^{\tau}}-\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{10}}\gg\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{10^{4}}}.

Thus for
z
being in the set of (
4.56
) and
\bm{n}\neq\pm\frac{\bm{l}_{0}}{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\|z+\bm{n}\cdot\bm{\omega}\pm\theta_{0}\|_{\mathbb{T}}\geq\|\bm{n}\cdot\bm{\omega}\pm\theta_{0}\|_{\mathbb{T}}-|z|\gg\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{10^{4}}}.

Hence for
\bm{n}\in(\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}-\bm{k})\setminus\left\{\pm\frac{\bm{l}_{0}}{2}\right\},
we have

\displaystyle|v(z+\bm{n}\cdot\bm{\omega})-E|>\kappa_{1}\cdot\delta_{0}^{2\times\frac{1}{10^{4}}}\gg\varepsilon.

Using Neumann series argument concludes

\displaystyle\left\|\left((\mathcal{M}_{1}(z))_{(\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}-\bm{k})\setminus\left\{\pm\frac{\bm{l}_{0}}{2}\right\}}\right)^{-1}\right\|<\delta_{0}^{-3\times\frac{1}{10^{4}}}.

(4.57)

Thus by Lemma
A.1
,
(\mathcal{M}_{1}(z))^{-1}
is controlled by the Schur complement of
(\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}-\bm{k})\setminus\left\{\pm\frac{\bm{l}_{0}}{2}\right\}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{1}(z)

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{M}_{1}(z))_{\left\{\pm\frac{\bm{l}_{0}}{2}\right\}}-\left(\vphantom{\left((\mathcal{M}_{1}(z))_{(\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}-\bm{k})\setminus\left\{\pm\frac{\bm{l}_{0}}{2}\right\}}\right)^{-1}}\mathcal{R}_{\left\{\pm\frac{\bm{l}_{0}}{2}\right\}}\mathcal{M}_{1}(z)\mathcal{R}_{(\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}-\bm{k})\setminus\left\{\pm\frac{\bm{l}_{0}}{2}\right\}}\right.

\displaystyle\ \ \left.\times\left((\mathcal{M}_{1}(z))_{(\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}-\bm{k})\setminus\left\{\pm\frac{\bm{l}_{0}}{2}\right\}}\right)^{-1}\mathcal{R}_{(\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}-\bm{k})\setminus\left\{\pm\frac{\bm{l}_{0}}{2}\right\}}\mathcal{M}_{1}(z)\mathcal{R}_{\left\{\pm\frac{\bm{l}_{0}}{2}\right\}}\right).

Then by (
1.2
), (
4.57
) and (
A.22
), we get

\displaystyle\max_{\bm{x}\in\left\{\pm\frac{\bm{l}_{0}}{2}\right\}}\sum_{\bm{y}\in\left\{\pm\frac{\bm{l}_{0}}{2}\right\}}|\mathcal{S}_{1}(z)(\bm{x},\bm{y})|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\bm{x}\in\left\{\pm\frac{\bm{l}_{0}}{2}\right\}}\sum_{\bm{y}\in\left\{\pm\frac{\bm{l}_{0}}{2}\right\}}\left|(\mathcal{M}_{1}(z))_{\left\{\pm\frac{\bm{l}_{0}}{2}\right\}}(\bm{x},\bm{y})\right|

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ +2(D(\alpha))^{2}\cdot\varepsilon^{2}\delta_{0}^{-\frac{3}{10^{4}}}

\displaystyle\leq 2|v|_{R}+\delta_{0}<4|v|_{R},

(4.58)

and

\displaystyle\det\mathcal{S}_{1}(z)

\displaystyle=\det\left((\mathcal{M}_{1}(z))_{\left\{\pm\frac{\bm{l}_{0}}{2}\right\}}\right)+O(\varepsilon^{2}\delta_{0}^{-\frac{3}{10^{4}}})

\displaystyle=\left(v\left(z+\frac{\bm{l}_{0}}{2}\cdot\bm{\omega}\right)-E\right)\left(v\left(z-\frac{\bm{l}_{0}}{2}\cdot\bm{\omega}\right)-E\right)+O(\varepsilon^{2}\delta_{0}^{-\frac{3}{10^{4}}}).

When
\bm{l}_{0}=\bm{0}
, the argument simplifies considerably, so we omit this case. For
\bm{l}_{0}\neq\bm{0}
, combining (
4.54
) with (
4.56
) yields

\displaystyle\left\|z+\frac{\bm{l}_{0}}{2}\cdot\bm{\omega}-\theta_{0}\right\|_{\mathbb{T}}

\displaystyle\geq\|\bm{l}_{0}\cdot\bm{\omega}\|_{\mathbb{T}}-\left\|\frac{\bm{l}_{0}}{2}\cdot\bm{\omega}+\theta_{0}\right\|_{\mathbb{T}}-|z|

\displaystyle>\frac{\gamma}{(100N_{1}^{10})^{\tau}}-\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{100}}-\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{10^{3}}}

\displaystyle>\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{10^{4}}},

and

\displaystyle\left\|z-\frac{\bm{l}_{0}}{2}\cdot\bm{\omega}+\theta_{0}\right\|_{\mathbb{T}}

\displaystyle\geq\|\bm{l}_{0}\cdot\bm{\omega}\|_{\mathbb{T}}-\left\|\frac{\bm{l}_{0}}{2}\cdot\bm{\omega}+\theta_{0}\right\|_{\mathbb{T}}-|z|

\displaystyle>\frac{\gamma}{(100N_{1}^{10})^{\tau}}-\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{100}}-\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{10^{3}}}

\displaystyle>\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{10^{4}}}.

Let
z_{1}
satisfy

\displaystyle z_{1}\equiv\frac{\bm{l}_{0}}{2}\cdot\bm{\omega}+\theta_{0}\ (\text{mod}\ \mathbb{Z}),\ |z_{1}|=\left\|\frac{\bm{l}_{0}}{2}\cdot\bm{\omega}+\theta_{0}\right\|_{\mathbb{T}}<\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{100}}.

(4.59)

Then

\displaystyle|\det\mathcal{S}_{1}(z)|

\displaystyle\geq\left\|z+\frac{\bm{l}_{0}}{2}\cdot\bm{\omega}-\theta_{0}\right\|_{\mathbb{T}}\cdot\left\|z-\frac{\bm{l}_{0}}{2}\cdot\bm{\omega}+\theta_{0}\right\|_{\mathbb{T}}

\displaystyle\ \ \cdot|(z-z_{1})(z+z_{1})+r_{1}(z)|

\displaystyle\geq\delta_{0}^{\frac{2}{10^{4}}}|(z-z_{1})(z+z_{1})+r_{1}(z)|,

where
r_{1}(z)
is an analytic function in the set of (
4.56
) with

\displaystyle|r_{1}(z)|\ll\varepsilon\ll\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{10^{3}}}.

(4.60)

By Rouché’s theorem, the equation

\displaystyle(z-z_{1})(z+z_{1})+r_{1}(z)=0

has exactly two roots
\theta_{1}
and
\theta_{1}^{\prime}
in the region defined by (
4.56
), which are small perturbations of
\pm z_{1}
. Suppose, for contradiction, that both

\displaystyle|z_{1}-\theta_{1}|>|r_{1}(\theta_{1})|^{\frac{1}{2}}\ \text{and}\ |z_{1}+\theta_{1}|>|r_{1}(\theta_{1})|^{\frac{1}{2}}.

This would imply

\displaystyle|r_{1}(\theta_{1})|=|z_{1}-\theta_{1}|\cdot|z_{1}+\theta_{1}|>|r_{1}(\theta_{1})|,

which is impossible. Therefore, without loss of generality, we may assume

\displaystyle|z_{1}-\theta_{1}|\leq|r_{1}(\theta_{1})|^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}.

We observe that the zero sets coincide:

\displaystyle\left\{|z|\leq\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{10^{3}}}:\ \det\mathcal{M}_{1}(z)=0\right\}=\left\{|z|\leq\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{10^{3}}}:\ \det\mathcal{S}_{1}(z)=0\right\}

and that
\det\mathcal{M}_{1}(z)
is an even function (see Lemma
A.2
). Consequently, we must have

\displaystyle\theta_{1}^{\prime}=-\theta_{1}.

Furthermore, from (
4.59
) and (
4.60
), we obtain for
|z|=\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{10^{3}}}
:

\displaystyle\frac{|r_{1}(z)-r_{1}(\theta_{1})|}{|(z-z_{1})(z+z_{1})+r_{1}(\theta_{1})|}\leq 2\varepsilon^{2}\delta_{0}^{-\frac{2}{10^{3}}}.

Combined with the identity
\theta_{1}^{2}-z_{1}^{2}+r_{1}(\theta_{1})=0
, this yields:

\displaystyle\frac{|(z-z_{1})(z+z_{1})+r_{1}(z)|}{|(z-\theta_{1})(z+\theta_{1})|}

\displaystyle=\frac{|(z-z_{1})(z+z_{1})+r_{1}(z)|}{|(z-z_{1})(z+z_{1})+r_{1}(\theta_{1})|}

\displaystyle\in\left[1-2\varepsilon^{2}\delta_{0}^{-\frac{2}{10^{3}}},1+2\varepsilon^{2}\delta_{0}^{-\frac{2}{10^{3}}}\right].

The maximum modulus principle then gives:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\leq\frac{|(z-z_{1})(z+z_{1})+r_{1}(z)|}{|(z-\theta_{1})(z+\theta_{1})|}\leq 2.

Consequently, for
z
in the region (
4.56
), we establish the determinant bound:

\displaystyle|\det\mathcal{S}_{1}(z)|\geq\frac{1}{2}\delta_{0}^{\frac{2}{10^{4}}}\|z-\theta_{1}\|_{\mathbb{T}}\cdot\|z+\theta_{1}\|_{\mathbb{T}}.

(4.61)

Applying Cramer’s rule with Lemma
3.3
, (
4.58
), and (
4.61
) yields the inverse estimate:

\displaystyle\|(\mathcal{S}_{1}(z))^{-1}\|=\frac{\|(\mathcal{S}_{1}(z))^{*}\|}{|\det\mathcal{S}_{1}(z)|}\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\|z-\theta_{1}\|_{\mathbb{T}}^{-1}\cdot\|z+\theta_{1}\|_{\mathbb{T}}^{-1}.

(4.62)

Combining (
4.57
), (
4.62
), and Lemma
A.1
, we derive the fundamental bound:

\displaystyle\|(\mathcal{M}_{1}(z))^{-1}\|

\displaystyle<4\left(1+\left\|\left((\mathcal{M}_{1}(z))_{(\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}-\bm{k})\setminus\left\{\pm\frac{\bm{l}_{0}}{2}\right\}}\right)^{-1}\right\|\right)^{2}(1+\|(\mathcal{S}_{1}(z))^{-1}\|)

\displaystyle<\delta_{0}^{-2}\|z-\theta_{1}\|_{\mathbb{T}}^{-1}\cdot\|z+\theta_{1}\|_{\mathbb{T}}^{-1}.

(4.63)

Thus for (
4.54
), these estimates (
4.62
) and (
4.2
) hold for all
z
in the periodic domain:

\displaystyle\{z\in\mathbb{C}:\ \|z\|_{\mathbb{T}}\leq\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{10^{3}}}\}

due to the 1-periodicity of
\mathcal{M}_{1}(z)
. Finally, for
\bm{k}\in P_{1}
via (
4.55
), we obtain:

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}}^{-1}\|

\displaystyle=\|(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}))^{-1}\|

\displaystyle<\delta_{0}^{-2}\|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}-\theta_{1}\|_{\mathbb{T}}^{-1}\cdot\|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}+\theta_{1}\|_{\mathbb{T}}^{-1}.

For the case of

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\bm{l}_{0}}{2}\cdot\bm{\omega}+\theta_{0}-\frac{1}{2}\right\|_{\mathbb{T}}<\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{100}},

(4.64)

we have for
\bm{k}\in P_{1}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}-\frac{1}{2}\right\|_{\mathbb{T}}<3\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{100}}.

(4.65)

Consider

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{1}(z):=\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}-\bm{k}}(z)=\left((v(z+\bm{n}\cdot\bm{\omega})-E)\delta_{\bm{n},\bm{n}^{\prime}}+\varepsilon\mathcal{W}\right)_{\bm{n}\in\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}-\bm{k}}

in

\displaystyle\left\{z\in\mathbb{C}:\ \left|z-\frac{1}{2}\right|\leq\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{10^{3}}}\right\}.

(4.66)

An analogous argument demonstrates that the equation
\det\mathcal{M}_{1}(z)=0
has two roots
\theta_{1}
and
\theta^{\prime}_{1}
within the region defined by (
4.66
), such that estimates (
4.58
)–(
4.2
) remain valid for all
z
in (
4.66
). Consequently, under condition (
4.64
), these estimates (
4.58
)–(
4.2
) extend to the shifted domain:

\displaystyle\left\{z\in\mathbb{C}:\ \left\|z-\frac{1}{2}\right\|_{\mathbb{T}}\leq\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{10^{3}}}\right\}.

Furthermore, for any
\bm{k}\in P_{1}
through (
4.65
), we derive the
\ell^{2}
-norm bound:

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}}^{-1}\|

\displaystyle=\|(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}))^{-1}\|

\displaystyle<\delta_{0}^{-2}\|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}-\theta_{1}\|_{\mathbb{T}}^{-1}\cdot\|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}+\theta_{1}\|_{\mathbb{T}}^{-1}.

(4.67)

We have thus established the desired norm estimates for
\|\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}}^{-1}\|
in both cases
(\bm{C}1)_{0}
and
(\bm{C}2)_{0}
. For each
\bm{k}\in P_{1}
, we define the subset
A_{\bm{k}}^{1}\subset\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{1}
as follows:

\displaystyle A_{\bm{k}}^{1}:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lc}\{\bm{k}\},&\text{Case}\ (\bm{C}1)_{0}\\
\{\bm{o}\}\cup\{\bm{o}^{*}\},&\text{Case}\ (\bm{C}2)_{0}\end{array}\right.,

where in Case
(\bm{C}2)_{0}
, the vector
\bm{k}
admits the decomposition
\bm{k}=\frac{1}{2}(\bm{o}+\bm{o}^{*})
for some
\bm{o}\in O_{1}
.

Step 2

:
Off-diagonal estimates of
\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}}^{-1}
.

The main result of this step is Theorem
4.5
, which establishes that despite the presence of singular 0-scale sites within the block
\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}
, the Green’s function
\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}}^{-1}
remains controllable provided no 1-scale singular sites exist. Recalling

\displaystyle\delta_{1}=\delta_{0}^{10^{5\rho^{\prime}}}

and

\displaystyle Q_{1}^{\pm}=\{\bm{k}\in P_{1}:\ \|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}\pm\theta_{1}\|_{\mathbb{T}}<\delta_{1}\},\ Q_{1}=Q_{1}^{+}\cup Q_{1}^{-},

we have

Theorem 4.5
.

For
\bm{k}\in P_{1}\setminus Q_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}}^{-1}(\bm{x},\bm{y})|<e^{-\alpha^{\prime}_{0}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|)}\ \text{for $\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|\geq\frac{\tilde{\zeta_{1}}}{10}$},

(4.68)

where
\alpha^{\prime}_{0}=\frac{3}{4}\alpha\left(1-\frac{20\times 10^{5\rho^{\prime}}}{\alpha\log^{\rho-\rho^{\prime}}N_{1}}\right)
is defined in (
4.22
).

Proof.

From our construction, we have

\displaystyle Q_{0}\subset\bigcup_{\bm{k}\in P_{1}}A_{\bm{k}}^{1}\subset\bigcup_{\bm{k}\in P_{1}}2\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{1}.

Thus

\displaystyle(\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}\setminus 2\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{1})\cap Q_{0}=\emptyset,

which shows that
\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}\setminus 2\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{1}
is
0
-good. Since (
4.67
) and
\bm{k}\notin Q_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}}^{-1}\|<\delta_{0}^{-2}\|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}-\theta_{1}\|_{\mathbb{T}}^{-1}\cdot\|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}+\theta_{1}\|_{\mathbb{T}}^{-1}<\delta_{0}^{-2}\delta_{1}^{-2}<\delta_{1}^{-3}.

(4.69)

To obtain the desired estimates, we divide the remaining proof into several cases.

Case 1
:

\bm{x}\in 2\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{1}
or
\bm{y}\in 2\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{1}
. Without loss of generality, we assume that
\bm{y}\in 2\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{1}
. From
\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|\geq\frac{\tilde{\zeta_{1}}}{10}
and
{\rm diam}(2\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{1})\ll\tilde{\zeta_{1}}
, we have
\bm{x}\in\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}\setminus 2\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{1}
. Since
\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}\setminus 2\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{1}
is
0
-good and (
4.33
), there is some
\bm{x}^{\prime}\in 2\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{1}
such that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}}^{-1}(\bm{x},\bm{y})|\leq(\#(\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}\setminus 2\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{1}))\cdot e^{-\frac{3}{4}\alpha\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{x}^{\prime}\|)}\cdot|\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}}^{-1}(\bm{x}^{\prime},\bm{y})|\cdot\delta_{0}^{-3}.

(4.70)

Next, we will extract
\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|)
from
\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{x}^{\prime}\|)
. Since
\bm{x}^{\prime},\bm{y}\in 2\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{1}
,
\|\bm{y}-\bm{x}^{\prime}\|\leq 4\zeta_{1}
. From
\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|\geq\frac{\tilde{\zeta}_{1}}{10}\gg 4\zeta_{1}\gg 1
and (
3.2
), we have

\displaystyle\ \ \log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{x}^{\prime}\|)\geq\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|-4\zeta_{1})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\ \ \left(1-2\rho\frac{4\zeta_{1}}{(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|)\log(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|)}\right)\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\ \ \left(1-\frac{80\rho\zeta_{1}}{\tilde{\zeta}_{1}\log N_{1}}\right)\log^{\rho}(\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|+1).

(4.71)

Therefore, from (
4.69
)–(
4.71
),
1<\rho^{\prime}<\rho<\rho^{\prime}+1
and
\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|\geq\frac{\tilde{\zeta}_{1}}{10}
, we get

\displaystyle|\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}}^{-1}(\bm{x},\bm{y})|

\displaystyle\leq(2\tilde{\zeta}_{1}+1)^{d}\cdot e^{-\frac{3}{4}\alpha\left(1-\frac{80\rho\zeta_{1}}{\tilde{\zeta}_{1}\log N_{1}}\right)\log^{\rho}(\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|+1)}\cdot\delta_{0}^{-5}\cdot\delta_{1}^{-2}

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\frac{3}{4}\alpha\left(1-\frac{6\times 10^{5\rho^{\prime}}}{\alpha\log^{\rho-\rho^{\prime}}N_{1}}\right)\log^{\rho}(\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|+1)}.

Case 2
:

\bm{x}\in\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}\setminus 2\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{1}
and
\bm{y}\in\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}\setminus 2\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{1}
. If
\bm{z}\in\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}\setminus\left(2\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{1}\bigcup\Lambda_{\frac{1}{2}N_{1}}(\bm{y})\right)
, there is a
0
-good set
\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}\cap\Lambda_{\frac{1}{2}N_{1}}(\bm{z})
such that

\displaystyle\bm{z}\in\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}\cap\Lambda_{\frac{1}{2}N_{1}}(\bm{z})\text{ and }\bm{y}\in\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}\setminus\Lambda_{\frac{1}{2}N_{1}}(\bm{z}).

For
\bm{z}\in\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}
, we can define

\displaystyle\hat{\bm{z}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\bm{z},&\bm{z}\in 2\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{1}\bigcup\Lambda_{\frac{1}{2}N_{1}}(\bm{y}),\\
\bm{z}^{\prime},&\bm{z}\in\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}\setminus\left(2\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{1}\bigcup\Lambda_{\frac{1}{2}N_{1}}(\bm{y})\right),\end{array}\right.

by Corollary
4.4
with
\bm{v}=\bm{y}
and
\Lambda^{\prime}=\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}\cap\Lambda_{\frac{1}{2}N_{1}}(\bm{z})
.

Let
\bm{x}_{0}:=\bm{x}
and
\bm{x}_{l+1}=\hat{\bm{x}}_{l}
,
l\geq 0
. For given
\{\bm{x}_{l}\}_{l\in\mathbb{N}}
, we define
l_{1}\geq 1
to be the smallest integer so that
\bm{x}_{l_{1}}\in 2\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{1}\bigcup\Lambda_{\frac{1}{2}N_{1}}(\bm{y})
. We then have

\displaystyle\bm{x}_{i}\in\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}\setminus\left(2\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{1}\bigcup\Lambda_{\frac{1}{2}N_{1}}(\bm{y})\right)\text{ for }0\leq i<l_{1}.

We also divide the discussion into 3 cases:

Case 2-1
:

l_{1}>\left[\frac{\alpha\log^{\rho}(\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|+1)+4\times 10^{5\rho^{\prime}}\log^{\rho^{\prime}}(N_{1}+1)}{\alpha\left(1-\frac{6\times 10^{5\rho^{\prime}}}{\alpha\log^{\rho-\rho^{\prime}}N_{1}}\right)\log^{\rho}\left(\frac{N_{1}}{2}+1\right)}\right]+1:=l^{*}
. Since
\bm{x}_{i}\in\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}\setminus\left(2\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{1}\bigcup\Lambda_{\frac{1}{2}N_{1}}(\bm{y})\right)
for
0\leq i<l_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\bm{x}_{i+1}=\bm{x}^{\prime}_{i}\in\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}\setminus\Lambda_{\frac{1}{2}N_{1}}(\bm{x}_{i})\ \text{(cf. Corollary \ref{ite0g})}

and then

\displaystyle\|\bm{x}_{i+1}-\bm{x}_{i}\|\geq\frac{N_{1}}{2}\ \text{for $0\leq i<l_{1}$.}

Thus, from (
4.3
), (
4.34
) and (
4.69
), we get

\displaystyle|\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}}^{-1}(\bm{x},\bm{y})|

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{i=0}^{l^{*}-1}\left(e^{-\frac{3}{4}\alpha\left(1-\frac{6\times 10^{5\rho^{\prime}}}{\alpha\log^{\rho-\rho^{\prime}}N_{1}}\right)\log^{\rho}(\|\bm{x}_{i+1}-\bm{x}_{i}\|+1)}\right)|\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}}^{-1}(\bm{x}_{l^{*}},\bm{y})|

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\frac{3}{4}\alpha\cdot l^{*}\left(1-\frac{6\times 10^{5\rho^{\prime}}}{\alpha\log^{\rho-\rho^{\prime}}N_{1}}\right)\log^{\rho}\left(\frac{N_{1}}{2}+1\right)}\delta_{1}^{-3}

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\frac{3}{4}\alpha\cdot l^{*}\left(1-\frac{6\times 10^{5\rho^{\prime}}}{\alpha\log^{\rho-\rho^{\prime}}N_{1}}\right)\log^{\rho}\left(\frac{N_{1}}{2}+1\right)}\cdot e^{3\times 10^{5\rho^{\prime}}\log^{\rho^{\prime}}(N_{1}+1)}

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\frac{3}{4}\alpha\log^{\rho}(\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|+1)}.

Case 2-2:

l_{1}\leq l^{*}
and
\bm{x}_{l_{1}}\in\Lambda_{\frac{1}{2}N_{1}}(\bm{y})
. According to
\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|\geq\frac{\tilde{\zeta}_{1}}{10}
,
N_{1}\gg 1
and
1<\rho^{\prime}<\rho
, we obtain

\displaystyle l^{*}

\displaystyle=\left[\frac{\alpha\log^{\rho}(\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|+1)+4\times 10^{5\rho^{\prime}}\log^{\rho^{\prime}}(N_{1}+1)}{\alpha\left(1-\frac{6\times 10^{5\rho^{\prime}}}{\alpha\log^{\rho-\rho^{\prime}}N_{1}}\right)\log^{\rho}\left(\frac{N_{1}}{2}+1\right)}\right]+1

\displaystyle\leq\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|).

(4.72)

Then from (
3.1
), (
4.34
), (
4.69
) and
\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|\geq\frac{\tilde{\zeta}_{1}}{10}
, we have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}}^{-1}(\bm{x},\bm{y})|

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{i=0}^{l_{1}-1}\left(e^{-\frac{3}{4}\alpha\left(1-\frac{6\times 10^{5\rho^{\prime}}}{\alpha\log^{\rho-\rho^{\prime}}N_{1}}\right)\log^{\rho}(\|\bm{x}_{i+1}-\bm{x}_{i}\|+1)}\right)|\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}}^{-1}(\bm{x}_{l_{1}},\bm{y})|

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\frac{3}{4}\alpha\left(1-\frac{6\times 10^{5\rho^{\prime}}}{\alpha\log^{\rho-\rho^{\prime}}N_{1}}\right)\left(\log^{\rho}(\|\bm{x}_{l_{1}}-\bm{x}\|+1)-C(\rho)\log^{\rho}l_{1}\right)}\delta_{1}^{-3}

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\frac{3}{4}\alpha\left(1-\frac{6\times 10^{5\rho^{\prime}}}{\alpha\log^{\rho-\rho^{\prime}}N_{1}}\right)\left(\log^{\rho}(\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|-\|\bm{x}_{l_{1}}-\bm{y}\|+1)-C(\rho)\log^{\rho}l_{1}\right)}

\displaystyle\ \ \cdot e^{3\times 10^{5\rho^{\prime}}\log^{\rho^{\prime}}(N_{1}+1)}.

(4.73)

By
\bm{x}_{l_{1}}\in\Lambda_{\frac{1}{2}N_{1}}(\bm{y})
,
\|\bm{x}_{l_{1}}-\bm{y}\|\leq\frac{N_{1}}{2}
. Since
\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|\geq\frac{\tilde{\zeta}_{1}}{10}\gg N_{1}\gg 1
and (
3.2
), we can get

\displaystyle\ \ \log^{\rho}(\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|-\|\bm{x}_{l_{1}}-\bm{y}\|+1)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\ \ \log^{\rho}\left(\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|-\frac{N_{1}}{2}+1\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\ \ \left(1-\rho\frac{N_{1}}{(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|)\log(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|)}\right)\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\ \ \left(1-\frac{10\rho N_{1}}{\tilde{\zeta}_{1}\log N_{1}}\right)\log^{\rho}(\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|+1).

(4.74)

From (
4.72
), (
4.74
),
\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|\geq\frac{\tilde{\zeta}_{1}}{10}\gg 1
and
1<\rho^{\prime}<\rho<\rho^{\prime}+1
, we get

\displaystyle\ \ \log^{\rho}(\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|-\|\bm{x}_{l_{1}}-\bm{y}\|+1)-C(\rho)\log^{\rho}l_{1}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\ \ \left(1-\frac{10^{5\rho^{\prime}}}{\alpha\log^{\rho-\rho^{\prime}}N_{1}}\right)\log^{\rho}(\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|+1)

and then

\displaystyle\ \ \left(1-\frac{10^{5\rho^{\prime}}}{\alpha\log^{\rho-\rho^{\prime}}N_{1}}\right)\left(\log^{\rho}(\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|-\|\bm{x}_{l_{1}}-\bm{y}\|+1)-C(\rho)\log^{\rho}l_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\ \ \left(1-\frac{3\times 10^{5\rho^{\prime}}}{\alpha\log^{\rho-\rho^{\prime}}N_{1}}\right)\log^{\rho}(\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|+1).

(4.75)

Since
\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|\geq\frac{\tilde{\zeta}_{1}}{10}\gg N_{1}\gg 1
, (
4.73
) and (
4.75
), we have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}}^{-1}(\bm{x},\bm{y})|

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\frac{3}{4}\alpha\left(1-\frac{10\times 10^{5\rho^{\prime}}}{\alpha\log^{\rho-\rho^{\prime}}N_{1}}\right)\log^{\rho}(\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|+1)}\cdot e^{3\times 10^{5\rho^{\prime}}\log^{\rho^{\prime}}(N_{1}+1)}

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\frac{3}{4}\alpha\left(1-\frac{20\times 10^{5\rho^{\prime}}}{\alpha\log^{\rho-\rho^{\prime}}N_{1}}\right)\log^{\rho}(\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|+1)}.

Case 2-3
:

l_{1}\leq l^{*}
and
\bm{x}_{l_{1}}\in 2\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{1}
. From a similar argument of (
4.73
), we obtain

\displaystyle|\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}}^{-1}(\bm{x},\bm{y})|

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\frac{3}{4}\alpha\left(1-\frac{6\times 10^{5\rho^{\prime}}}{\alpha\log^{\rho-\rho^{\prime}}N_{1}}\right)\left(\log^{\rho}(\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|-\|\bm{x}_{l_{1}}-\bm{y}\|+1)-C(\rho)\log^{\rho}l_{1}\right)}

\displaystyle\ \ \cdot|\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}}^{-1}(\bm{x}_{l_{1}},\bm{y})|

(4.76)

By Corollary
4.4
(since
\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}\setminus 2\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{1}
is
0
-good), there is some
\bm{y}^{\prime}\in 2\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{1}
such that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}}^{-1}(\bm{x}_{l_{1}},\bm{y})|\leq(\#\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1})\cdot e^{-\frac{3}{4}\alpha\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{y}-\bm{y}^{\prime}\|)}\cdot|\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}}^{-1}(\bm{x}_{l_{1}},\bm{y}^{\prime})|\cdot\delta_{0}^{-3}.

(4.77)

According to
