Appendix A

Proof of Lemma
3.2
.

Direct computation shows

\displaystyle\log^{\rho}(1+x-y)=\left(1+\frac{\log\left(1-\frac{y}{1+x}\right)}{\log(1+x)}\right)^{\rho}\log^{\rho}(1+x).

(A.1)

Since
x>y
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\log\left(1-\frac{y}{1+x}\right)}{\log(1+x)}>-1,

which combines
(1+z)^{\rho}\geq 1+\rho z

(\rho>1,z>-1)
showing that

\displaystyle\left(1+\frac{\log\left(1-\frac{y}{1+x}\right)}{\log(1+x)}\right)^{\rho}\geq 1+\rho\frac{\log\left(1-\frac{y}{1+x}\right)}{\log(1+x)}.

(A.2)

From
0<2y<1+x
and
\log(1-z)\geq-2z

\left(0<z<\frac{1}{2}\right)
, we can get

\displaystyle\log\left(1-\frac{y}{1+x}\right)\geq-\frac{2y}{1+x}.

(A.3)

Combining (
A.1
)–(
A.3
) gives

\displaystyle\log^{\rho}(1+x-y)\geq\left(1-2\rho\frac{y}{(1+x)\log(1+x)}\right)\log^{\rho}(1+x).

We finish this proof.
∎

Proof of Lemma
3.3
.

By Hadamard’s inequality, we have for any
\bm{i},\bm{j}\in\Lambda,

\displaystyle|\langle\delta_{\bm{i}},\mathcal{S}_{\Lambda}^{*}\delta_{\bm{j}}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{\bm{l}\neq\bm{i}}\left(\sum_{\bm{k}\neq\bm{j}}|\langle\delta_{\bm{l}},\mathcal{S}_{\Lambda}\delta_{\bm{k}}\rangle|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{\bm{l}\neq\bm{i}}\left(\sum_{\bm{k}\neq\bm{j}}|\langle\delta_{\bm{l}},\mathcal{S}_{\Lambda}\delta_{\bm{k}}\rangle|\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sup_{\bm{x}\in\Lambda}\sum_{\bm{y}\in\Lambda}|\langle\delta_{\bm{x}},\mathcal{S}_{\Lambda}\delta_{\bm{y}}\rangle|\right)^{\#\Lambda-1}.

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{S}_{\Lambda}^{*}\|

\displaystyle\leq\left(\left(\sup_{\bm{i}\in\Lambda}\sum_{\bm{j}\in\Lambda}|\langle\delta_{\bm{i}},\mathcal{S}_{\Lambda}^{*}\delta_{\bm{j}}\rangle|\right)\cdot\left(\sup_{\bm{j}\in\Lambda}\sum_{\bm{i}\in\Lambda}|\langle\delta_{\bm{i}},\mathcal{S}_{\Lambda}^{*}\delta_{\bm{j}}\rangle|\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq(\#\Lambda)\cdot\left(\sup_{\bm{x}\in\Lambda}\sum_{\bm{y}\in\Lambda}|\langle\delta_{\bm{x}},\mathcal{S}_{\Lambda}\delta_{\bm{y}}\rangle|\right)^{\#\Lambda-1}.

We complete this proof.
∎

Proof of Lemma
3.4
.

For any fixed
\bm{y}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
and any cut off
K>0
, the
multiplication operator

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}=e^{\lambda^{\prime}\min\{\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|),K\}}\delta_{\bm{x},\bm{x}^{\prime}},\ \bm{x}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}

(A.4)

is bounded and invertible on
\ell^{2}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
for any
\lambda^{\prime}\in(0,+\infty)
. The Combes-Thomas
estimate is based on the observation that for any
\bm{x}
with
\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|)\leq K
and
any
z\notin\sigma(\mathcal{A})\cup\sigma(\mathcal{M}\mathcal{A}\mathcal{M}^{-1})
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(z)(\bm{x},\bm{y})e^{\lambda^{\prime}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|)}

\displaystyle=\langle\delta_{\bm{x}},\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{A}-z)^{-1}\mathcal{M}^{-1}\delta_{\bm{y}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\delta_{\bm{x}},(\mathcal{M}\mathcal{A}\mathcal{M}^{-1}-z)^{-1}\delta_{\bm{y}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\delta_{\bm{x}},(\mathcal{A}+\mathcal{B}-z)^{-1}\delta_{\bm{y}}\rangle,

(A.5)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}:=\mathcal{M}\mathcal{A}\mathcal{M}^{-1}-\mathcal{A}.

(A.6)

For a proof of (
3.4
), this will be done by estimating the

\ell^{2}
-norm of
\mathcal{B}
. By Schur’s test, we need to estimate
\mathcal{B}(\bm{x},\bm{x}^{\prime})
for all
\bm{x},\bm{x}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. We divide the discussion into two cases:

Case 1
:

\bm{x}=\bm{x}^{\prime}
or
\min\{\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|),\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}^{\prime}-\bm{y}\|)\}\geq K
. In this case, direct computation shows that
\mathcal{B}(\bm{x},\bm{x}^{\prime})=0
.

Case 2
:

\bm{x}\neq\bm{x}^{\prime}
and
\max\{\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|),\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}^{\prime}-\bm{y}\|)\}<K
. From (
3.1
), (
A.4
) and (
A.6
), we have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{B}(\bm{x},\bm{x}^{\prime})|

\displaystyle=|\mathcal{A}(\bm{x},\bm{x}^{\prime})|\cdot\left|e^{\lambda^{\prime}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|)-\lambda^{\prime}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}^{\prime}-\bm{y}\|)}-1\right|

\displaystyle\leq|\mathcal{A}(\bm{x},\bm{x}^{\prime})|\cdot\left(e^{\left|\lambda^{\prime}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|)-\lambda^{\prime}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}^{\prime}-\bm{y}\|)\right|}-1\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2e^{\lambda^{\prime}C(\rho)\log^{\rho}2}|\mathcal{A}(\bm{x},\bm{x}^{\prime})|\cdot e^{\lambda^{\prime}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{x}^{\prime}\|)}.

Case 3
:

\min\{\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|),\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}^{\prime}-\bm{y}\|)\}<K
and
\max\{\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|),\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}^{\prime}-\bm{y}\|)\}\geq K
. Without loss of generality, we assume that
\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|)<K
and
\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}^{\prime}-\bm{y}\|)\geq K
. Also by (
3.1
), (
A.4
) and (
A.6
), we can get

\displaystyle|\mathcal{B}(\bm{x},\bm{x}^{\prime})|

\displaystyle=|\mathcal{A}(\bm{x},\bm{x}^{\prime})|\cdot\left|e^{\lambda^{\prime}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|)-\lambda^{\prime}K}-1\right|

\displaystyle\leq|\mathcal{A}(\bm{x},\bm{x}^{\prime})|\cdot\left(e^{\left|\lambda^{\prime}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|)-\lambda^{\prime}K\right|}-1\right)

\displaystyle=|\mathcal{A}(\bm{x},\bm{x}^{\prime})|\cdot\left(e^{\lambda^{\prime}K-\lambda^{\prime}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|)}-1\right)

\displaystyle\leq|\mathcal{A}(\bm{x},\bm{x}^{\prime})|\cdot\left(e^{\lambda^{\prime}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}^{\prime}-\bm{y}\|)-\lambda^{\prime}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|)}-1\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2e^{\lambda^{\prime}C(\rho)\log^{\rho}2}|\mathcal{A}(\bm{x},\bm{x}^{\prime})|\cdot e^{\lambda^{\prime}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{x}^{\prime}\|)}.

To sum up, we obtain
\mathcal{B}(\bm{x},\bm{x})=0
and

\displaystyle|\mathcal{B}(\bm{x},\bm{x}^{\prime})|\leq 2e^{\lambda^{\prime}C(\rho)\log^{\rho}2}|\mathcal{A}(\bm{x},\bm{x}^{\prime})|\cdot e^{\lambda^{\prime}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{x}-\bm{x}^{\prime}\|)},\ \bm{x}\neq\bm{x}^{\prime},

(A.7)

which is independent from
K
. According to the self-adjointness of
\mathcal{A}
, (
3.3
) and (
A.7
), we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{B}\|\leq 2e^{\lambda^{\prime}C(\rho)\log^{\rho}2}\cdot S_{\lambda^{\prime}}\leq 2e^{\lambda C(\rho)\log^{\rho}2}\cdot S_{\lambda}<\infty

for all
\lambda^{\prime}\leq\lambda
. The spectrum of
\mathcal{B}+\mathcal{A}
has distance at most
\|\mathcal{B}\|
from the
spectrum of
\mathcal{A}
. Hence
\mathcal{B}+\mathcal{A}-z
is invertible as long as
\mathscr{D}={\rm dist}(z,\sigma(\mathcal{A}))>\|B\|
, for which

\displaystyle\|(\mathcal{B}+\mathcal{A}-z)^{-1}\|\leq\frac{1}{\mathscr{D}-\|\mathcal{B}\|}\leq\frac{1}{\mathscr{D}-2e^{\lambda^{\prime}C(\rho)\log^{\rho}2}\cdot S_{\lambda^{\prime}}}.

(A.8)

In view of (
A.5
) and the fact that
K
was arbitrary, we have thus proved (
3.4
).
∎

Proof of Lemma
4.7
.

Using the resolvent identity implies

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(\bm{u},\bm{v})=-\varepsilon\sum_{\bm{w}\in\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}\atop\bm{w}^{\prime}\in\Lambda\setminus\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}}\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}}^{-1}(\bm{u},\bm{w})\cdot\mathcal{W}(\bm{w},\bm{w}^{\prime})\cdot\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(\bm{w}^{\prime},\bm{v}),

and then

\displaystyle|\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(\bm{u},\bm{v})|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\bm{w}\in\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}\atop\bm{w}^{\prime}\in\Lambda\setminus\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}}\left|\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}}^{-1}(\bm{u},\bm{w})\cdot\mathcal{W}(\bm{w},\bm{w}^{\prime})\cdot e^{\frac{\alpha}{10}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{w}-\bm{w}^{\prime}\|)}\cdot\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(\bm{w}^{\prime},\bm{v})\right|

\displaystyle\ \ \times\left(\sum_{\bm{w}\in\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}\atop\bm{w}^{\prime}\in\Lambda\setminus\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}}e^{-\frac{\alpha}{10}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{w}-\bm{w}^{\prime}\|)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\bm{w}\in\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}\atop\bm{w}^{\prime}\in\Lambda\setminus\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}}\left|\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}}^{-1}(\bm{u},\bm{w})\cdot\mathcal{W}(\bm{w},\bm{w}^{\prime})\cdot e^{\frac{\alpha}{10}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{w}-\bm{w}^{\prime}\|)}\cdot\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(\bm{w}^{\prime},\bm{v})\right|

\displaystyle\ \ \times(\#\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1})\cdot D\left(\frac{\alpha}{10}\right),

(A.9)

where
D\left(\frac{\alpha}{10}\right)
is defined in (
2.1
). Since
\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}
and
\Lambda\setminus\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}
are finite sets, there are
\bm{u}^{*}\in\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}
and
\bm{u}^{\prime}\in\Lambda\setminus\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}
such that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}}^{-1}(\bm{u},\bm{u}^{*})\cdot\mathcal{W}(\bm{u}^{*},\bm{u}^{\prime})\cdot e^{\frac{\alpha}{10}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{u}^{*}-\bm{u}^{\prime}\|)}\cdot\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(\bm{u}^{\prime},\bm{v})|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \ \max_{\bm{w}\in\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}\atop\bm{w}^{\prime}\in\Lambda\setminus\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}}|\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}}^{-1}(\bm{u},\bm{w})\cdot\mathcal{W}(\bm{w},\bm{w}^{\prime})\cdot e^{\frac{\alpha}{10}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{w}-\bm{w}^{\prime}\|)}\cdot\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(\bm{w}^{\prime},\bm{v})|.

(A.10)

Let
X:=\Lambda_{\frac{\tilde{\zeta}_{1}}{9}}(\bm{k})\subset\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}
. We will divide the discussion into two cases,
\bm{u}^{*}\in X
and
\bm{u}^{*}\in\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}\setminus X
.

Case 1
:

\bm{u}^{*}\in X
. Since
\bm{k}\in\widetilde{P}_{1}\subset P_{1}\setminus Q_{1}
(cf. (
4.69
)) and (
1.2
), we can get

\displaystyle\ \ |\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}}^{-1}(\bm{u},\bm{u}^{*})\cdot\mathcal{W}(\bm{u}^{*},\bm{u}^{\prime})\cdot e^{\frac{\alpha}{10}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{u}^{*}-\bm{u}^{\prime}\|)}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \ 2\delta_{0}^{-3}\delta_{1}^{-2}\cdot e^{-\frac{9}{10}\alpha\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{u}-\bm{u}^{\prime}\|)}.

(A.11)

Case 2
:

\bm{u}^{*}\in\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}\setminus X
. In this case,
\|\bm{u}^{*}-\bm{u}\|\geq\frac{\tilde{\zeta}_{1}}{10}
. From (
4.68
), (
3.1
) and (
1.2
), we have

\displaystyle\ \ |\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}}^{-1}(\bm{u},\bm{u}^{*})\cdot\mathcal{W}(\bm{u}^{*},\bm{u}^{\prime})\cdot e^{\frac{\alpha}{10}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{u}^{*}-\bm{u}^{\prime}\|)}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \ e^{-\alpha^{\prime}_{0}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{u}-\bm{u}^{*}\|)}\cdot e^{-\frac{9}{10}\alpha\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{u}^{*}-\bm{u}^{\prime}\|)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \ e^{-\alpha^{\prime}_{0}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{u}-\bm{u}^{\prime}\|)+\alpha^{\prime}_{0}C(\rho)\log^{\rho}2}.

(A.12)

Combining (
A.11
), (
A.12
) and
\delta_{1}\ll 1
gives

\displaystyle\ \ |\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}}^{-1}(\bm{u},\bm{u}^{*})\cdot\mathcal{W}(\bm{u}^{*},\bm{u}^{\prime})\cdot e^{\frac{\alpha}{10}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{u}^{*}-\bm{u}^{\prime}\|)}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \ 2\delta_{0}^{-3}\delta_{1}^{-2}\cdot e^{-\alpha^{\prime}_{0}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{u}-\bm{u}^{\prime}\|)}.

(A.13)

By (
A.9
), (
A.10
), (
A.13
),
\bm{u}^{\prime}\in\Lambda\setminus\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}
(
\|\bm{u}-\bm{u}^{\prime}\|\geq\frac{\tilde{\zeta}_{1}}{3}
),
\#(\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1})\leq(10N_{1}^{100}+1)^{d}
, (
4.3
) and
\delta_{1}\ll 1
, we obtain

\displaystyle|\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(\bm{u},\bm{v})|

\displaystyle\leq(\#(\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{1}))\cdot e^{-\alpha^{\prime}_{0}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{u}-\bm{u}^{\prime}\|)}\cdot|\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(\bm{u}^{\prime},\bm{v})|\cdot\delta_{1}^{-3}

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\alpha^{\prime\prime}_{0}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{u}-\bm{u}^{\prime}\|)}\cdot|\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(\bm{u}^{\prime},\bm{v})|

(A.14)

where
\alpha^{\prime\prime}_{0}=\frac{3}{4}\alpha\left(1-\frac{30\times 10^{5\rho^{\prime}}}{\alpha\log^{\rho-\rho^{\prime}}N_{1}}\right)
.

We finish this proof.
∎

Proof of Lemma
4.9
.

Using the resolvent identity implies

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(\bm{u},\bm{v})=-\varepsilon\sum_{\bm{w}\in\Lambda^{\prime}\atop\bm{w}^{\prime}\in\Lambda\setminus\Lambda^{\prime}}\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda^{\prime}}^{-1}(\bm{u},\bm{w})\cdot\mathcal{W}(\bm{w},\bm{w}^{\prime})\cdot\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(\bm{w}^{\prime},\bm{v}),

and then

\displaystyle|\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(\bm{u},\bm{v})|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\bm{w}\in\Lambda^{\prime}\atop\bm{w}^{\prime}\in\Lambda\setminus\Lambda^{\prime}}\left|\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda^{\prime}}^{-1}(\bm{u},\bm{w})\cdot\mathcal{W}(\bm{w},\bm{w}^{\prime})\cdot e^{\frac{\alpha}{10}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{w}-\bm{w}^{\prime}\|)}\cdot\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(\bm{w}^{\prime},\bm{v})\right|

\displaystyle\ \ \times\left(\sum_{\bm{w}\in\Lambda^{\prime}\atop\bm{w}^{\prime}\in\Lambda\setminus\Lambda^{\prime}}e^{-\frac{\alpha}{10}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{w}-\bm{w}^{\prime}\|)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\bm{w}\in\Lambda^{\prime}\atop\bm{w}^{\prime}\in\Lambda\setminus\Lambda^{\prime}}\left|\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda^{\prime}}^{-1}(\bm{u},\bm{w})\cdot\mathcal{W}(\bm{w},\bm{w}^{\prime})\cdot e^{\frac{\alpha}{10}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{w}-\bm{w}^{\prime}\|)}\cdot\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(\bm{w}^{\prime},\bm{v})\right|

\displaystyle\ \ \times(\#\Lambda^{\prime})\cdot D\left(\frac{\alpha}{10}\right),

(A.15)

where
D\left(\frac{\alpha}{10}\right)
is defined in (
2.1
). Since
\Lambda^{\prime}
and
\Lambda\setminus\Lambda^{\prime}
are finite sets, there are
\bm{u}^{*}\in\Lambda^{\prime}
and
\bm{u}^{\prime}\in\Lambda\setminus\Lambda^{\prime}
such that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda^{\prime}}^{-1}(\bm{u},\bm{u}^{*})\cdot\mathcal{W}(\bm{u}^{*},\bm{u}^{\prime})\cdot e^{\frac{\alpha}{10}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{u}^{*}-\bm{u}^{\prime}\|)}\cdot\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(\bm{u}^{\prime},\bm{v})|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \ \max_{\bm{w}\in\Lambda^{\prime}\atop\bm{w}^{\prime}\in\Lambda\setminus\Lambda^{\prime}}|\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda^{\prime}}^{-1}(\bm{u},\bm{w})\cdot\mathcal{W}(\bm{w},\bm{w}^{\prime})\cdot e^{\frac{\alpha}{10}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{w}-\bm{w}^{\prime}\|)}\cdot\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(\bm{w}^{\prime},\bm{v})|.

(A.16)

If
\|\bm{u}^{*}-\bm{u}\|\leq 10\tilde{\zeta}_{t}
, from
\Lambda^{\prime}
is
t
-good (cf. (
4.23
)) and (
1.2
), we can get

\displaystyle\ \ |\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda^{\prime}}^{-1}(\bm{u},\bm{u}^{*})\cdot\mathcal{W}(\bm{u}^{*},\bm{u}^{\prime})\cdot e^{\frac{\alpha}{10}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{u}^{*}-\bm{u}^{\prime}\|)}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \ \delta_{t}^{-3}\cdot e^{-\frac{9}{10}\alpha\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{u}-\bm{u}^{\prime}\|)}.

(A.17)

If
\|\bm{u}^{*}-\bm{u}\|>10\tilde{\zeta}_{t}
, since
\Lambda^{\prime}
is
t
-good (cf. (
4.24
)), (
1.2
) and (
3.1
), we have

\displaystyle\ \ |\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda^{\prime}}^{-1}(\bm{u},\bm{u}^{*})\cdot\mathcal{W}(\bm{u}^{*},\bm{u}^{\prime})\cdot e^{\frac{\alpha}{10}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{u}^{*}-\bm{u}^{\prime}\|)}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \ e^{-\alpha_{t}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{u}-\bm{u}^{*}\|)}\cdot e^{-\frac{9}{10}\alpha\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{u}^{*}-\bm{u}^{\prime}\|)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \ e^{-\alpha_{t}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{u}-\bm{u}^{\prime}\|)+\alpha_{t}C(\rho)\log^{\rho}2}.

(A.18)

Combining (
A.17
), (
A.18
) and
\delta_{t}\ll 1
gives

\displaystyle\ \ |\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda^{\prime}}^{-1}(\bm{u},\bm{u}^{*})\cdot\mathcal{W}(\bm{u}^{*},\bm{u}^{\prime})\cdot e^{\frac{\alpha}{10}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{u}^{*}-\bm{u}^{\prime}\|)}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \ \delta_{t}^{-3}\cdot e^{-\alpha_{t}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{u}-\bm{u}^{\prime}\|)}.

(A.19)

By (
A.15
), (
A.16
), (
A.19
) and
\delta_{t}\ll 1
, we obtain

\displaystyle|\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(\bm{u},\bm{v})|\leq(\#\Lambda^{\prime})\cdot e^{-\alpha_{t}\log^{\rho}(1+\|\bm{u}-\bm{u}^{\prime}\|)}\cdot|\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(\bm{u}^{\prime},\bm{v})|\cdot\delta_{t}^{-3}.

(A.20)

We finish this proof.
∎

Lemma A.1

(Schur complement lemma)
.

Let
\Lambda_{1}
and
\Lambda_{2}
be finite subsets of
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
with
\Lambda_{1}\cap\Lambda_{2}=\emptyset
. Suppose
\mathcal{A}\in\mathbf{M}_{\Lambda_{1}}^{\Lambda_{1}}
,
\mathcal{B}\in\mathbf{M}_{\Lambda_{2}}^{\Lambda_{1}}
,
\mathcal{C}\in\mathbf{M}_{\Lambda_{1}}^{\Lambda_{2}}
,
\mathcal{D}\in\mathbf{M}_{\Lambda_{2}}^{\Lambda_{2}}
and

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathcal{A}&\mathcal{B}\\
\mathcal{C}&\mathcal{D}\end{array}\right)\in\mathbf{M}_{\Lambda_{1}\cup\Lambda_{2}}^{\Lambda_{1}\cup\Lambda_{2}}.

Assume further that
\mathcal{A}
is invertible and
\|\mathcal{B}\|,\|\mathcal{C}\|\leq 1
. Then we have

(1)

\displaystyle\det\mathcal{M}=\det\mathcal{A}\cdot\det\mathcal{S},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}=\mathcal{D}-\mathcal{C}\mathcal{A}^{-1}\mathcal{B}\in\mathbf{M}_{\Lambda_{2}}^{\Lambda_{2}}

is called the Schur complement of
\mathcal{A}
.

(2)

\mathcal{M}
is invertible iff
\mathcal{S}
is invertible and

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{S}^{-1}\|\leq\|\mathcal{M}^{-1}\|<4(1+\|\mathcal{A}^{-1}\|)^{2}(1+\|\mathcal{S}^{-1}\|).

(A.21)

Proof of Lemma
A.1
.

(1) Since
\mathcal{A}
is invertible, we have

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathcal{I}_{\Lambda_{1}}&\bm{0}\\
-\mathcal{C}\mathcal{A}^{-1}&\mathcal{I}_{\Lambda_{2}}\end{array}\right)\mathcal{M}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathcal{I}_{\Lambda_{1}}&-\mathcal{A}^{-1}\mathcal{B}\\
\bm{0}&\mathcal{I}_{\Lambda_{2}}\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathcal{A}&\bm{0}\\
\bm{0}&\mathcal{S}\end{array}\right),

which implies

\displaystyle\det\mathcal{M}=\det\mathcal{A}\cdot\det\mathcal{S}.

(2) Direct computation shows

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{-1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathcal{A}^{-1}+\mathcal{A}^{-1}\mathcal{B}\mathcal{S}^{-1}\mathcal{C}\mathcal{A}^{-1}&-\mathcal{A}^{-1}\mathcal{B}\mathcal{S}^{-1}\\
-\mathcal{S}^{-1}\mathcal{C}\mathcal{A}^{-1}&\mathcal{S}^{-1}\end{array}\right),

which combines
\|\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}\|\leq\|\mathcal{A}\|\cdot\|\mathcal{B}\|
implying (
A.21
).
∎

Lemma A.2
.

Let
\bm{l}\in\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}^{d}
and let
\Lambda\subset\mathbb{Z}^{d}+\bm{l}
be a finite set which is symmetrical about the origin (i.e.,
\bm{n}\in\Lambda\Leftrightarrow-\bm{n}\in\Lambda
). Then

\displaystyle\det(\mathcal{T}(z))_{\Lambda}=\det((v(z+\bm{n}\cdot\bm{\omega})-E)\delta_{\bm{n},\bm{n}^{\prime}}+\varepsilon\mathcal{W})_{\Lambda}

is an even function of
z
.

Proof.

Define the unitary map

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{\Lambda}:\ell^{2}(\Lambda)\rightarrow\ell^{2}(\Lambda)\ \text{with}\ (\mathcal{U}_{\Lambda}\psi)(\bm{n})=\psi(-\bm{n}).

Then

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(\mathcal{T}(z))_{\Lambda}\mathcal{U}_{\Lambda}=((v(z-\bm{n}\cdot\bm{\omega})-E)\delta_{\bm{n},\bm{n}^{\prime}}+\varepsilon\mathcal{W})_{\Lambda}=(\mathcal{T}(-z))_{\Lambda},

which implies

\displaystyle\det(\mathcal{T}(z))_{\Lambda}=\det(\mathcal{T}(-z))_{\Lambda}.

We completes this proof.
∎

Lemma A.3
.

Let
\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}:\ell^{2}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})\rightarrow\ell^{2}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
be linear operators and let
\Lambda
be a finite subset of
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. If
\sup_{\bm{x}\in\Lambda}\sum_{\bm{y}\in\Lambda}|\langle\delta_{\bm{x}},\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}\delta_{\bm{y}}\rangle|\leq M
and
\sup_{\bm{x}\in\Lambda}\sum_{\bm{y}\in\Lambda}|\langle\delta_{\bm{x}},\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda}\delta_{\bm{y}}\rangle|\leq\epsilon
, then

\displaystyle\left|\det(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}+\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda})-\det\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}\right|\leq\varepsilon(\#\Lambda)^{2}(M+\varepsilon)^{\#\Lambda-1}.

(A.22)

Proof.

Let
f(t)=\det(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}+t\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda})
. Then

\displaystyle f^{\prime}(t)={\rm tr}(\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}+t\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda})^{*}).

Since
\sup_{\bm{x}\in\Lambda}\sum_{\bm{y}\in\Lambda}|\langle\delta_{\bm{x}},\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}\delta_{\bm{y}}\rangle|\leq M
and
\sup_{\bm{x}\in\Lambda}\sum_{\bm{y}\in\Lambda}|\langle\delta_{\bm{x}},\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda}\delta_{\bm{y}}\rangle|\leq\epsilon
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{x}\in\Lambda}\sum_{\bm{y}\in\Lambda}|\langle\delta_{\bm{x}},(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}+t\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda})\delta_{\bm{y}}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\bm{x}\in\Lambda}\sum_{\bm{y}\in\Lambda}|\langle\delta_{\bm{x}},\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}\delta_{\bm{y}}\rangle|+|t|\cdot\sup_{\bm{x}\in\Lambda}\sum_{\bm{y}\in\Lambda}|\langle\delta_{\bm{x}},\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda}\delta_{\bm{y}}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq M+\epsilon|t|.

By Lemma
3.3
, we get for any
\bm{i},\bm{j}\in\Lambda
,

\displaystyle|\langle\delta_{\bm{i}},(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}+t\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda})^{*}\delta_{\bm{j}}\rangle|\leq

\displaystyle\ \left(\sup_{\bm{x}\in\Lambda}\sum_{\bm{y}\in\Lambda}|\langle\delta_{\bm{x}},(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}+t\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda})\delta_{\bm{y}}\rangle|\right)^{\#\Lambda-1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ (M+\epsilon|t|)^{\#\Lambda-1}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle|f^{\prime}(t)|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\bm{i}\in\Lambda}|\langle\delta_{\bm{i}},\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}+t\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda})^{*}\delta_{\bm{i}}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon(\#\Lambda)^{2}\max_{\bm{i},\bm{j}\in\Lambda}|\langle\delta_{\bm{i}},(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}+t\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda})^{*}\delta_{\bm{j}}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon(\#\Lambda)^{2}(M+\epsilon|t|)^{\#\Lambda-1}.

According to the mean-value theorem, we obtain for some
\xi\in(0,1),

\displaystyle|\det(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}+\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda})-\det\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}|=|f(1)-f(0)|=|f^{\prime}(\xi)|\leq\epsilon(\#\Lambda)^{2}(M+\epsilon)^{\#\Lambda-1}.

This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma A.4
.

For any set
B\subseteq\mathbb{Z}^{d},
there is a
s
-regular deformation
B^{*}
satisfying

B\subseteq B^{*}\subseteq\Lambda_{50N^{100}_{s}}(B)=\{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}:{\rm dist}(\bm{x},B)\leq 50N^{100}_{s}\}.

Proof.

We start from

\displaystyle J_{0,0}=B.

Inductively define

\displaystyle J_{r,0}\subsetneq J_{r,1}\subsetneq\cdot\subsetneq J_{r,t_{r}}:=J_{r+1,0},

where

\displaystyle J_{r,t+1}=J_{r,t}\cup\left(\bigcup_{\{\bm{k}\in P_{s-r}:\ \Lambda_{2N_{s-r}^{100}}(\bm{k})\cap J_{r,t}\}}\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s-r}\right),

and
t_{r}
is the largest integer satisfying the
\subsetneq
relationship. Thus,

\displaystyle\bm{k}\in P_{s-r},\ \Lambda_{2N_{s-r}^{100}}(\bm{k})\cap J_{r+1,0}\neq\emptyset\Rightarrow\Lambda_{2N_{s-r}^{5}}(\bm{k})\subset J_{r+1,0}.

(A.23)

We claim that
t_{r}\leq 2
,
0\leq r\leq s-1
. Otherwise, there exist three different points
\bm{k}_{1},\bm{k}_{2},\bm{k}_{3}\in P_{s-r}
such that

\displaystyle J_{r,0}\cap\Lambda_{2N_{s-r}^{100}}(\bm{k}_{1})\neq\emptyset,\ \Lambda_{2N_{s-r}^{100}}(\bm{k}_{1})\cap\Lambda_{2N_{s-r}^{100}}(\bm{k}_{2})\neq\emptyset,\ \Lambda_{2N_{s-r}^{100}}(\bm{k}_{2})\cap\Lambda_{2N_{s-r}^{100}}(\bm{k}_{3})\neq\emptyset.

Thus
\max(\|\bm{k}_{1}-\bm{k}_{2}\|,\|\bm{k}_{2}-\bm{k}_{3}\|,\|\bm{k}_{1}-\bm{k}_{3}\|)\leq 10N_{s}^{100}
. According to (
4.6
) and (
4
), then two of the three points
\bm{k}_{i}
must satisfy

\displaystyle\|(\bm{k}_{i}-\bm{k}_{j})\cdot\bm{\omega}\|_{\mathbb{T}}<6\delta_{s-r}^{\frac{1}{100}},\ i\neq j

which implies
\|\bm{k}_{i}-\bm{k}_{j}\|>\left(\frac{\gamma}{6\delta_{s-r}^{\frac{1}{100}}}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tau}}\gg N_{s-r}^{100}
, a contradiction. Thus, we have shown

\displaystyle J_{r+1,0}=J_{r,t_{r}}\subset\Lambda_{10N_{s-r}^{100}}(J_{r,0}).

SInce

\displaystyle\sum_{r=0}^{s-1}20N_{s-r}^{100}<50N_{s}^{100},

we find
J_{s,0}
to satisfy

\displaystyle B=J_{0,0}\subset J_{s,0}\subset\Lambda_{50N_{s}^{100}}(B).

(A.24)

Assume that for some
\bm{k}\in P_{s^{\prime}}

(1\leq s^{\prime}\leq s)
,
J_{s,0}\cap\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s^{\prime}}\neq\emptyset
. From
\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s^{\prime}}\subset\Lambda_{1.5N_{s^{\prime}}^{100}}(\bm{k})
, we obtain

\displaystyle J_{s,0}\cap\Lambda_{1.5N_{s^{\prime}}^{100}}(\bm{k})\neq\emptyset.

Recalling (
A.24
), we have

\displaystyle J_{s,0}\subset\Lambda_{50N_{s^{\prime}-1}^{100}}(J_{s-s^{\prime}+1,0}).

From
50N_{s^{\prime}-1}^{100}\ll 0.5N_{s^{\prime}}^{100}
, it follows that

\displaystyle J_{s-s^{\prime}+1,0}\cap\Lambda_{2N_{s^{\prime}}^{100}}(\bm{k})\neq\emptyset.

By (
A.23
), we get

\displaystyle\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s^{\prime}}\subset\Lambda_{2N_{s^{\prime}}^{100}}(\bm{k})\neq\emptyset\subset J_{s-s^{\prime}+1,0}\subset J_{s,0}.

Then
B^{*}=J_{s,0}
is the set set that satisfies all conditions.
∎
